nāṉkām aṅkam:  aintām kaḷam muṟṟiṟṟu.
(kalittuṟai) 
	aritā niṉaittataṉ aṅkaṅkaḷ yāvum aḻintapiṉṉum
puriyēporuḷeṉap pōṟṟiya jīvakaṉ puntiyeṉṉē!
piriyāta cārpu peyarntu virākam piṟantiṭiṉum
teriyātu taṉṉilai āṇavam ceyyum tiṟañciṟitē!

nāṉkām aṅkam muṟṟiṟṟu.

āciriyappā	12	-kku aṭi	1297
vañcippā	1	-kku aṭi	14
kalittāḻicai	3	-kku aṭi 	12
kalittuṟai	1	-kku aṭi	4
āka, aṅkam 1-kku pā. 17kku aṭi	1327
aintām aṅkattiṉ viḷakkam
mutaṟ kaḷam
amaiccaṉ kuṭilaṉ, araṇmaṉaiyil cuntaramuṉivar amaitta curaṅka vaḻiyaik kaṇṭupiṭittu ataṉ vaḻiyāka iṟaṅkic ceṉṟāṉ. avvaḻi, kōṭṭaikkum, cēramaṉṉaṉ irunta pācaṟaikkum iṭaiyēyuḷḷa veḷiyiṭattil koṇṭupōy viṭṭatu. iravu nēram. kuṭilaṉukkup putiya yōcaṉai uṇṭāyiṟṟu. nērē cēraṉ irukkum pācaṟaikkuc ceṉṟu taṉ eṇṇattait terivittāl ataṟku avaṉ uṭaṉpaṭuvāṉ. āṇṭutōṟum tāmpiraparaṇi nīrum vēppan tārum aṉuppikkoṇṭēyiruntāl, avaṉ taṉṉaiyē aracaṉākkuvāṉ eṉṟu avaṉ cintittāṉ. “palatēvaṉukkum maṉōṉmaṇikkum tirumaṇam naṭantāl eṉṉa? naṭakkāviṭṭāl eṉṉa? ippoḻutē eṉṉai avaṉ etirttup pēcukiṟāṉ. paṭaivīrarkaḷ nammai veṟukkiṟārkaḷ. cēraṉaik kaṇṭu vaṇaṅki nayamākap pēciṉāl, avaṉ iṇaṅki viṭuvāṉ! āā !  namatu aṟivē aṟivu! ūḻ eṉṟum talaiviti eṉṟum pēcuvatu ellām vīṇpēccu! intac curaṅka vaḻi namakku nallatāka amaintatu” eṉṟu niṉaittuk koṇṭē naṭantāṉ. naṭantu,  cēraṉ pācaṟaikku arukil ceṉṟāṉ.
avvamayam cēramaṉṉaṉ puruṭōttamaṉ uṟakkam illāmal aṅkut taṉṉantaṉiyaṉāka ulāvikkoṇṭiruntāṉ. avaṉ taṉ kaṉavil aṭikkaṭi tōṉṟum naṅkaiyaip paṟṟic cintittuk koṇṭē ulavukiṟāṉ. avaṉaik kaṇṭa kuṭilaṉ viyappaṭaintāṉ.  “ivaṉ maṉitaṉ allaṉ tēvaṉō? kantaruvaṉpōlak kāṇap paṭukiṟāṉ!” eṉṟu eṇṇiṉāṉ. cēra aracaṉ puruṭōttamaṉ mella naṭantu avviṭam vantapōtu kuṭilaṉaik kaṇṭāṉ. ayalāṉ eṉa aṟintu, “ yār? uṉ peyar eṉṉa?” eṉṟu kēṭṭāṉ. 
“aṭiyēṉ, kuṭilaṉ” eṉṟāṉ amaiccaṉ. 
“inta nērattil iṅku varak kāraṇam eṉṉa?”
“aracē! taṅkaḷiṭam oru vārttai colla vantēṉ. kumpiṭap pōṉa teyvam kuṟukkē vantatupōla tāṅkaḷ etirppaṭṭīrkaḷ !” eṉṟāṉ kuṭilaṉ. 
“vanta kāriyam eṉṉa? viraivil col?” eṉṟāṉ puruṭōt tamaṉ.
“aracē! taṅkaḷ pukaḻ ulakameṅkum paraviyuḷḷatu. eṅkaḷ nāṭṭaiyum tāṅkaḷē aracāḷvatu takuti. iṉṟu naṭanta pōril maṉa millāmalē nāṉ pōr ceytatu tāṅkaḷ aṟintatē. makkaḷ, taṅkaḷ pukaḻai eṇṇit taṅkaḷaiyē aracarāka ēṟkavirukkiṉṟaṉar. āṉāl, pāṇṭiyaṉ avarkaḷaip pōr ceyyat tūṇṭukiṟāṉ” eṉṟāṉ.
kuṭilaṉ, cūtāka ētō karutukiṟāṉ eṉṟu aṟinta cēraṉ, “nallatu! ataṉāl uṉakku vēṇṭiyatu eṉṉa? col”eṉṟāṉ.
kuṭilaṉ kūṟukiṟāṉ: “āṇṭakaiyē! pōril māṇṭavar pōka, mīṇṭavar uyiraiyēṉum kāttaruḷa vēṇṭum. vīṇāka makkaḷ māṇṭupōṉatu eṉ maṉattait tuṉpuṟuttukiṟatu. maṟṟavarkaḷai yellām pōril maṭiyātapaṭi kāttaruḷa vēṇṭum!” 
“uṉ aracaṉiṭam ēṉ itaic collavillai?” eṉṟu kēṭṭāṉ puruṭōttamaṉ.
“collip payaṉ eṉṉa? avar col puttiyum kēḷār. aruḷ uḷḷam illātavar. iṉṟu mālaiyil tāṅkaḷ viṭṭa tūtu  vārttaiyaiyum ēṟṟukkoṇṭārillai. makkaḷaip pōrk kaḷattil aṉuppik koṉṟu nāṭṭaic cuṭukāṭākkap pārkkiṟār. aṭiyēṉukku oru vārttai coṉṉāl, pāṇṭiyaṉaiyum kōṭṭaiyaiyum oru noṭiyil taṅkaḷ vacam oppuvikkiṟēṉ.”
itaik kēṭṭa cēraṉ, ‘pātakaṉ! vicuvāca kātakaṉ!’ eṉṟu taṉakkuḷ eṇṇikkoḷkiṟāṉ.
kuṭilaṉ mēlum toṭarntu collukiṟāṉ : “aracē pāṇṭiya aracaṉ taṅkaḷ kaivacamāṉāl, aṅkuḷḷavar oruvarum taṅkaḷai etirkkamāṭṭārkaḷ. tirunelvēli taṅkaḷukkuriya tāyviṭṭāl maturaiyum taṅkaḷukkuk kīḻppaṭṭuviṭum. pāṇṭiya nāṭu taṅkaḷ aṭikkīḻ otuṅkum. aracarperuma! aṭiyēṉukku araca patavi maṭṭum aruḷceyvīrāṉāl, tāṅkaḷ virumpiyatupōla nīrum tārum eṉ talaimēl cumantukoṇṭu taṅkaḷ vāyilil koṇṭuvantu taruvēṉ. irāmaṉ veṉṟa ilaṅkaiyai vipīṣaṇaṉ kāttatupōla pāṇṭināṭṭaik kāttiṭuvēṉ!” 
puruṭōttamaṉ, ivaṉ tantiracāli, cāmarttiyamākap pēcukiṟāṉ eṉṟu eṇṇikkoḷkiṟāṉ.
kuṭilaṉ, “aracaṉuṭaiya antappurantukkup pōka oruvarum aṟiyāta oru curaṅkavaḻi uṇṭu. avvaḻiyāyp pōṉāl aracaṉaic ciṟaippiṭikkalām” eṉṟāṉ.
puruṭōttamaṉ, “uṇmaitāṉā?” eṉṟu kēṭṭukkoṇṭē, “yār aṅkē....” eṉṟu aḻaittāṉ. caṟṟut tūrattiliruntu cēṉāpati aruḷvarataṉ viraintu vantu vaṇaṅkiṉāṉ, “kaikālkaḷukkut taḷaiyiṭa vilaṅkukaḷ koṇṭuvā” eṉṟāṉ puruṭōttamaṉ.
kuṭilaṉ: “aracarperumāṉē! aṭiyēṉ kūṟuvatu muḻuvatum uṇmai.” “curaṅkavaḻi eṅkē irukkiṟatu? nī avvaḻi yākattāṉ vantāyō?” eṉṟu kēṭṭāṉ cēraṉ.
“arukilēyē irukkiṟatu. avvaḻiyākattāṉ vantēṉ” eṉṟāṉ kuṭilaṉ.
aruḷvarataṉ cila vīrarkaḷuṭaṉ vilaṅkukaḷaik koṇṭu vara, cēraṉ kuṭilaṉaic cuṭṭikkāṭṭi, “pūṭṭuṅkaḷ!” eṉṟu āṇaiyiṭṭāṉ. vīrarkaḷ kuṭilaṉukku vilaṅku pūṭṭiṉārkaḷ.  “aracē! nāṉ ōṭa māṭṭēṉ. eṉakkēṉ  vilaṅku? aruḷkūrntu eṉakku vākkaḷiyu ṅkaḷ” eṉṟu vēṇṭiṉāṉ kuṭilaṉ.
cēraṉ, “vāyai mūṭu ! cēraṉ vañcamāka vellamāṭṭāṉ! pōrkkaḷattilē aracarkaḷai veṉṟu ciṟaippiṭippāṉ... nallatu, naṭa. “curaṅka vaḻiyaik kāṭṭuka” eṉṟu kaṭṭaḷai yiṭṭāṉ. kuṭilaṉ curaṅka vaḻiyaik kāṭṭi muṉnaṭakka, cēvakarkaḷum, aruḷvarataṉum, puruṭōttamaṉum piṉ toṭarntu ceṉṟaṉar. 
iraṇṭām kaḷam
araṇmaṉaiyil kaṉṉimāṭattil ūḻiyap peṇkaḷ taṅkaḷukkuḷ pēcikkoḷkiṟārkaḷ. aracaṉ, iḷavaraciyaip pala tēvaṉukku maṇamceyya yōcittiruppataik kūṟiyatum, ataṟku iḷavaraci virumpavillaiyāṉālum, aracaṉukku uḷḷa maṉattuṉpat taiyum nerukkaṭiyaiyum karuti, avaṉukku mēlum mēlum maṉa varuttam ēṟpaṭakkūṭātu eṉṟu karuti tirumaṇattiṟku icaintatum, aracaṉ ceṉṟavuṭaṉ iḷavaraci mūrccaiyaṭaintu viḻuntuviṭṭatum, piṟaku neṭunēram kaḻittut teḷinteḻuntatum ākiyavaṟṟaippaṟṟip pēcukiṉṟaṉar.
avvamayam, iḷavaraci maṉōṉmaṇi nīrāṭit tirumaṇattiṟ kuriya āṭaiyaṇikaḷai aṇintukoṇṭu vāṇiyuṭaṉ avviṭam varukiṟāḷ. ūḻiyap peṇkaḷ vēṟiṭam pōkiṉṟaṉar. iḷavaraci, tāṉ iraṇṭu varaṅkaḷai aracariṭam kēṭṭuk kaṭitam eḻutiyatākavum, av varaṅkaḷai aracar koṭuttatākavum kūṟukiṟāḷ. “vāṇi, uṉṉai naṭarācaṉukku maṇam ceyvikka aracar icain tuḷḷār. itō, avar eḻutiya icaivuk kaṭitam, pār” eṉṟu kūṟi, iḷavaraci, vāṇiyiṭam oru kaṭitattaik koṭukkiṟāḷ.
vāṇi, kaṭitattaip paṭittu, “eṉṉa ammā, itu? eṉakku itu cammatam illai. tāṅkaḷ maṉam varuntiyirukka, nāṉ maṇamceytu iṉpuṟuvatā? itu muṭiyātu!” eṉṟaṉaḷ.
“eṉ tantaiyiṉ maṉampōla naṭappatu eṉ kaṭamai inta nerukkaṭiyil avar iṣṭampōl naṭakkāviṭṭāl avar perun tuyar aṭaivār eṉṟāḷ” maṉōṉmaṇi.
“taṅkaḷaip palatēvaṉukkut tirumaṇam ceyvippatu pakalukkum iravukkum uḷḷa uṟavupōṉṟatu. kiḷiyaik kaḻukukku maṇam ceyvippatupōṉṟatu. taṅkaḷuṭaiya peruntuyarukkiṭaiyē eṉṉuṭaiya tirumaṇantāṉā peritu?” eṉṟu kūṟi maṟuttāḷ vāṇi.
maṉōṉmaṇi, “vāṇi ! varuntātē. eṉ tantaikku vanta tuṉpattaip pōkkuvataṟkākavē avaruṭaiya viruppappaṭi naṭantu avarukku makiḻcci uṇṭākkuvataṟkākavē it tirumaṇattai nāṉ ēṟṟukkoṇṭēṉ. maṟṟavarkaḷiṉ naṉmaikkākat tamatu cukaṅkaḷait tuṟappatutāṉ uṇmaiyāṉa tavam. uṉṉai naṭarājaṉukku maṇam ceyvikka vēṇṭum eṉṟum, nārāyaṇaraic ciṟai viṭuvikka vēṇṭum eṉṟum aracariṭam kēṭṭēṉ. avar avvitamē cammatam tantār” eṉṟu kūṟiṉāḷ.
vāṇi, “ammaṇi! tāṅkaḷ kaṉavu kaṇṭu kātalitta avarai  -  avar yārāyiṉum ākuka - avarait tāṅkaḷ tirumaṇam ceyyum nāḷē nāṉ tirumaṇam ceyyum naṉṉāḷ. atuvaraiyil nāṉ maṇam ceyyēṉ. taṅkaḷiṭam paṇi viṭai ceytukoṇṭiruppēṉ” eṉṟāḷ.
“vāṇi ! nī colvatu pētaimai. eṉ maṇam iṉṉum arai nāḻikaiyil naṭakkappōkiṟatu. itaṟkuḷ nāṉ kaṉaviṟ kaṇṭa nāyakaṉ eppaṭi varappōkiṟār? nī eṉṉiṭam koṇṭuḷḷa aṉpiṉāl ivvitam kūṟukiṟāy!” eṉṟāḷ maṉōṉmaṇi.
“kaṭavuḷ illai eṉṟāl ippaṭi ellām naṭakkaṭṭum. uṇṭu eṉṟāl avar tiruvaruḷ kiṭaikkaṭṭum” eṉṟāḷ vāṇi.
mūṉṟām kaḷam
araṇmaṉaiyil maṇa maṇṭapattil naḷḷiravil paṭṭam pakal pōla viḷakkukaḷ veḷiccan tarukiṉṟaṉa. maṇṭapam alaṅkarikkap paṭṭu aḻakākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. vīrarkaḷum mukkiyastarkaḷum kūṭiyirukkiṉṟaṉar. maṇṭapattil iraṇṭu tiraikaḷ kāṇap paṭukiṉṟaṉa. oru tirai maṇamakaḷukkāka; maṟṟoru tirai, curaṅka vāyilai maṟaippataṟkāka. pāṇṭiyaṉ maṇṭapattil nuḻaikiṟāṉ. avaṉaip piṉtoṭarntu cuntara muṉivar, karuṇākarar, niṣṭāparar, palatēvaṉ, naṭarājaṉ, nārāyaṇaṉ mutaliyōr varukiṉṟaṉar. aracaṉ ācaṉattil amarntu ellōraiyum amarumpaṭi kūṟukiṟāṉ. amaiccaṉākiya kuṭilaṉ aṅku illātatu kaṇṭu, “ummuṭaiya tantaiyār iṉṉum iṅku varātatu eṉṉa?” eṉṟu viyappuṭaṉ palatēvaṉaik kēṭkiṟāṉ.
“mālaiyil avaraip pārttēṉ; taṉiyē pōṉār eṉṟu kūṟukiṟārkaḷ” eṉṟāṉ palatēvaṉ.
“pāruṅkaḷ, kuṭilar paṭumpāṭu ! eppoḻutum nam kāriyamākavē kaṇṇāyirukkiṟār. namatu kāriyamākattāṉ avar pōyiruppār. ippaṭi uḻaippavar enta ulakattilum illai” eṉṟu kūṟi, muṉivaraip pārttu, “aṭikāḷ ! mukūrtta vēḷai vantatō? toṭaṅkalāmō?” eṉṟu kēṭkiṟāṉ.
avvēḷaiyil curaṅka vaḻiyāka aruḷvarataṉ mutaliyavaruṭaṉ puruṭōttamaṉ aṅku varukiṟāṉ. kuṭilaṉaip pattiramāyp pārttuk koḷḷac curaṅkattil cēvakariṭam viṭṭu, puruṭōttamaṉum cēṉāpati yum aṟaiyil nuḻaikiṉṟaṉar. tiraimaṟaivil iruntapaṭiyē, tiraikkappāl kāṇappaṭum kāṭcikaḷai viḷakku veḷiccattil naṉṟākak kāṇkiṉṟaṉar. puruṭōttamaṉ, cuntara muṉivarum naṭarājaṉum maṇṭapattil iruppataik kaṇṭu viyappaṭaikiṟāṉ. ‘iteṉṉa! mantirālōcaṉai naṭaipeṟukiṟatō! ... ... alaṅkāraṅ kaḷum mālaikaḷum iruppataip pārttāl maṇavaṟaipōla irukkiṟatu. nallatu; intat tiraimaṟaivil niṉṟu naṭappataip pārppōm !’ eṉṟu taṉakkuḷ eṇṇikkoḷkiṟāṉ.
antac camayattil pāṇṭiyaṉ kūṟukiṟāṉ: “aṭikaḷē! amaiccarkaḷē! vīrarkaḷē! kēḷuṅkaḷ. namatu iḷavaraciyaik kāppataṟkāka iṅkuk kūṭiyirukkiṟōm. iṉṟu kālai pōrk kaḷattil namakku ōr iḻukku nērntatu. anta iḻukkai nāḷaik kālaiyil pōrkkaḷattil veṟṟi koṇṭāvatu, uyir koṭut tāvatu nīkkuvōm. ataṟku muṉ namatu iḷavaracikkuc ceyya vēṇṭiya kaṭamaiyaic ceytu muṭikkavēṇṭum.
“kuṭilarai aṟiyātavar yār ? avaraippōla aṟivum cūḻcciyum uṇmaiyum uṟutiyum paktiyum cattiyamum uṭaiyavar vēṟu yār uḷḷaṉar ! taṉṉalam illāmal namakkāka uḻaikkiṉṟār. pāruṅkaḷ; ippōtuṅkūṭa inta naṭuiravil avar eṅkēyō ceṉṟu namakkākap pāṭupaṭukiṟār. ippaṭippaṭṭa amaiccaraip peṟṟatu namatu pākkiyamē (tiraimaṟaivil iruntu itaṉaik kēṭṭa puruṭōttamaṉ, ‘aiyō! kaṣṭam! ivvaracaṉ kaḷaṅka millātavaṉ ! kuṭilaṉiṉ turōkattai yaṟiyātavaṉ!”eṉṟu taṉakkuḷ kūṟikkoḷkiṟāṉ.) avaruṭaiya makaṉ palatēvaṉ vīram, aṟivu, āṟṟal, ūkkam mutaliyavaṟṟil ciṟantu viḷaṅkukiṟatai ellōrum aṟivīrkaḷ. namakku nērntirukkiṟa pōr nerukkaṭiyaik karutiyum, kuṭilaruṭaiya kuṭumpattukku nām kaṭamaip paṭṭiruppatu karutiyum namatu iḷavaraciyai palatēvarukkut tirumaṇam ceyvikka icaintuḷḷaḷōm. tirumaṇam muṭintavuṭaṉ maṇamakkaḷ muṉivar āciramattiṟ ceṉṟu taṅkuvārkaḷ. ataṟkākavē aṭikaḷ iṅkiruntu curaṅkavaḻi yoṉṟai amaittuḷḷār. iḷavaraci maṉōṉmaṇiyaip paṟṟiya kavalai tīrnta piṟaku, nāḷaik kālai pōrkkaḷam ceṉṟu pōr ceytu namatu māṉattaik kāppōm!” eṉṟu kūṟi, cakaṭarai nōkki, “ umatu karuttaik kūṟum” eṉṟu kūṟiṉāṉ.
“ellām caritāṉ ; maṇamakaṉ aracakulam alla... atu tāṉ...” eṉṟu iḻuttār cakaṭar. “kulattaippaṟṟi yōcippataiviṭa kuṇattaip paṟṟittāṉ karutavēṇṭum” eṉṟāṉ aracaṉ. “aracē ! maṉitarāl āvatu oṉṟumillai. ellām īcaṉ ceyal” eṉṟāṉ nārāyaṇaṉ. 
cakaṭarum maṟṟavarkaḷum ‘caricari’ eṉṟu kūṟic cammatam terivittaṉar. aracaṉ, iḷavaraciyai maṇa maṇṭapattiṉ tiraikkuḷ varumpaṭi aḻaippittāṉ. maṉōṉmaṇi, vāṇivāṇi kaṭavuḷ vāḻttup pāṭiṉāḷ.
cuntaramuṉivar, “maṉōṉmaṇī! iṅku varuka! mālaiyaik kaiyil eṭu. uṉ maṉatai iṟaivaṉ aṟivāṉ. avaṉ tiruvaruḷ iṉṉamum uṉṉaik kākkum” eṉṟu kūṟiṉār. maṉōṉmaṇi maṇa mālaiyaik kaiyil ēnti, vāṭiya mukattuṭaṉ uyir illāp pāvai pōla tiraikku veḷiyē varukiṟāḷ. atēcamayam etirilirunta tiraic cīlaiyi liruntu puruṭōttamaṉ tiraikku veḷiyē varukiṟāṉ. tāṉ kaṉavil kaṇṭu kātalitta uruvamē atu eṉṟu aṟintu viyappum makiḻcciyum poṅka maṉōṉmaṇi aṅku viraintu ceṉṟu mālaiyaip puruṭōttamaṉ kaḻuttil iṭukiṟāḷ. puruṭōttamaṉum, tāṉ kaṉavil kaṇṭu varum peṇ ivaḷ eṉṟu aṟintu viyappaṭaikiṟāṉ.
etirpārāta innikaḻcciyaik kaṇṭu ellōrum aticayik kiṉṟaṉar. 
cuntaramuṉivar, “maṅkalam uṇṭākuka!” eṉṟu maṇamakkaḷai vāḻttukiṟār.
palatēvaṉ, “piṭiyuṅkaḷ avaṉai! kolluṅkaḷ!” eṉṟu kūvukiṟāṉ. cuntara muṉivar avarkaḷait taṭuttu amaitiyuṇṭākku kiṟār. cēraṉ cēṉāpati aruḷvarataṉum avaṉuṭaiya vīrarkaḷum, puruṭōttamaṉaiyum maṉōṉmaṇiyaiyum cūḻntu niṉṟukāval purikiṉṟaṉar. ellōrum, “pātakaṉ ! paṭaiyuṭaṉ iṅkēyum vantāṉ” eṉkiṉṟaṉar. aruḷvarataṉ, vilaṅkuṭaṉ talaikuṉintu niṟkum kuṭilaṉaic cuṭṭikkāṭṭik kampīramāka, “itō uṅkaḷ pātakaṉ!” eṉṟu kūṟiṉāṉ.
jīvakaṉ, “kuṭilarē ! eṉṉa itu ! aṭikāḷ ! itu eṉṉa cūtu!” eṉṟāṉ. 
“poṟu ! poṟu ! viraivil aṟivōm” eṉṟār muṉivar.
puruṭōttamaṉ pēcuciṟāṉ : “nām cūtu oṉṟum ceyyavillai. cūtu ceytavaṉ ummuṭaiya amaiccaṉ ! curaṅka vaḻi iruppatai eṉṉiṭam kūṟi ivvaḻiyāka vantu umatu nakarattaip piṭikkumpaṭi emmai aḻaittāṉ. umatu aracaiyum nāṭṭaiyum taṉakkuk koṭukkumpaṭi eṉṉaik kēṭṭāṉ. ivaṉuṭaiya cūtaiyum turōkattaiyum umakkuc colli ummait teḷivikkavē ivaṉaic curaṅka vaḻiyāka nām aḻaittu vantōm. vanta iṭattil, maṇa nikaḻcciyaik kaṇṭōm. iḷavaraciyai etirpārātavitamākap peṟṟōm. iṉi umatu viruppam, ceṉṟu varukiṟēṉ. umakku viruppamāṉāl pōrkkaḷattil cantippōm.”
jīvakaṉ, “kuṭilarē! itu uṇmaiyā! collum!” eṉṟāṉ. kuṭilaṉ pēcāmal talaikuṉintu niṟkiṉṟāṉ. 
“ēṉ mauṉam ? kuṭilarē! uṇmaiyaik kūṟum!” eṉṟāṉ nārāyaṇaṉ. kuṭilaṉaip pārttu ellōrum, “aṭa pāvī!”eṉṟaṉar. 
cuntarar, “viṭuṅkaḷ!  ippōtu ēṉ vicāraṇai ? ellām kaṭavuḷ tiruvaruḷ... aracē! makaḷaiyum marukaṉaiyum vāḻttum” eṉṟār. ellōrum maṇamakkaḷai vāḻttukiṉṟaṉar.


aintām  aṅkam 
mutaṟ kaḷam
iṭam:	kōṭṭaikkum vañciyar pācaṟaikkum
	naṭuviluḷḷa veḷi.
kālam:	yāmam.
(kuṭilaṉ taṉiyē naṭakka)
(nēricai āciriyappā)
kuṭilaṉ:	(taṉimoḻi) 
		tirumaṇam keṭiṉum tīṅkilai īṅkiṉi.
	irucaram iṉṟi eppō riṭaiyum
	ēkār matiyōr. itilvaru kēṭeṉ?
	ākā vaḻiyum aṉṟitu. cēraṉai 
5	aṇaintavaṉ maṉakkōḷ uṇarntataṉ piṉṉar 
	curuṅkaiyiṉ taṉmai collutum oruṅkē.
	icaivaṉēṟ kāṭṭutum. iṉṟēl mīḷkutum.
	pacaiyilā maṉattaṉ!  paṇitalē virumpuvaṉ!
	pāṇṭinā ṭāḷavō paṭaiyeṭut tāṉivaṉ?
10	tūṇṭiṭu ciṉattaṉ; toḻutiṭil mīḷvaṉ.
	vēṇṭiya nīrum viḻaintatōr tārum
	pāṇṭil pāṇṭilā yāṇṭukaḷ tōṟum
	aṉupputum. kuṟaiveṉ ataṉil? ituvē
	maṉakkuṟai nīkku mārkkam. - vatuvai
15	pōyiṉeṉ? āyiṉeṉ? pēyaṉ nammakaṉ 
	eṭutteṟin tiṭuvaṉip pōtē nammoḻi.
	aṭuttanam paṭaiñarō pakaivar; avarnamaik 
	keṭuttanā raṇaṟkē kēḷoṭu kiḷaiñar,
	ātaliṉ iḵtē tītaṟu muṟuti ...
20	eṉṉainam ūkam! eṉṉainam ūkkam! 
	muṉṉaryām aṟiyā iṉṉanaṟ curuṅkaiyil
	tuṉṉiruḷ vaḻitaṉi toṭarntivaṇ cērntōm.
	ūkkamē pākkiyam. uṇarvilār vēṟu
	pākkiyam ūḻeṉap pakarvatel lāmpāḻ.
25	cākkiyam vēṟeṉ? cāttiyā cāttiyam 
	aṟikuṟi palavāl āyntaṟin tāṟṟum
	tiṟamuḷa ūkamē yōkam; aṉṟi 
		(naṭcattiraṅkaḷai nōkki)
		vāṉkā ṭataṉil vaṟitē cuḻalum
	mīṉkāḷ! vēṟum uḷatō viḷampīr?
30	matiyilā mākkaḷ vitiyeṉa nummēṟ
	cumattum cumaiyum tūṟṟum cummaiyum
	umakkiṭu peyarum uruvamum toḻilum
	amaikkum kuṇamum atilvaru vātamum.
	yuktiyum ūkamum paktiyum pakaimaiyum.
35	oṉṟaiyum nīvir uṇarīr! aḵteṉ? 
	veṉṟavar pācaṟai viḷaṅkuva taḵtō! 
	iṅkumaṟ ṟulāvuvaṉ yāvaṉ? poṅkukāl
	varuntoṟum cilamoḻi varuva.aḵtō
	tirumpiṉaṉ ! otuṅkuvam. terintumēṟ celkuvam.
(puruṭōttamaṉ taṉiyā yulāvi vara)
(kuṟaḷveṇ centuṟai)
puruṭōttamaṉ:  (pāṭa)
		uṇṇiṉaivil orupōtum
		ōyviṉṟik kalantiruntum
			uyirē eṉṟaṉ
	kaṇṇiṇaikaḷ orupōtum
		kaṇṭilavē niṉṉuruvam
			kāṭṭāy kāṭṭāy.		1
		avattaipala aṭaiyumaṉam
		aṉavaratam pucittiṭiṉum
			amirtē eṉṟaṉ
	cevittuḷaikaḷ aṟintilavē
		tittikkum niṉṉāmam
			ceppāy ceppāy.		2
		poṟikaḷaṟi yātuḷḷē
		pukumporuḷkaḷ ilaiyeṉpar
			poruḷē uṉṉai
	aṟiyavavā viyakaraṇam
		alamākka akattiruntāy
			accō accō.		3
		(puruṭōttamaṉ  caṟṟē akala)
(āciriyappāviṉ toṭarcci)
kuṭi: 		(taṉimoḻi)
	40	maṉitaṉ alaṉivaṉ! puṉitakan taruvaṉ!
	tēvarum uḷarō? yātō? aṟiyēṉ.
	iruḷelām oḷiviṭa ilaṅkiya uruvam
	maruḷtaru mataṉaṉ vaṭivē! mataṉaṟku 
	uruvilai eṉpar. ōcaiyum uruvum!
45	pāṭiya pāṭṭiṉ payaṉeṉ! aḵtō!
	nāṭi aṟikutum. naṉṟu naṉṟu.
		(puruṭōttamaṉ tirumpivara)
(kuṟaḷveṇ centuṟai - toṭarcci)
puru:		(pāṭa)
		pulaṉārak kāṇpatuvē
		poruḷeṉṉum pōtamilāp 
			puṉmai yōrkkiṅ
	kulavāteṉ uḷaniṟaiyum
		uṉatuṇmai uṇarttumvakai
			uṇṭē uṇṭē.		4
		pettamaṉak kaṟpitamē
		piṟaṅkuniṉai veṉappitaṟṟum
			pētai yōrkkōr
	yattaṉamaṟ ṟirukkaveṉṉuḷ
		eḻumuṉatu nilaiyuraippa
			teṉṉē yeṉṉē.		5
		tērviṭatteṉ uḷḷaniṟai
		teḷḷamutē uṉṉilaimai
			tērā tiṅṅaṉ 
	ūrviṭuttum pōrtoṭuttum
		uṉaiyakala niṉaittatumeṉ
			ūḻē ūḻē.			6
(āciriyappāviṉ toṭarcci)
kuṭi:		cēraṉē yāmitu ceppiṉōṉ. pōriṉil
	orupuṟam otuṅki aracaṉai akaṟṟi
	niṉṟatāṟ kaṇṭilēṉ. niṟainta kāmukaṉ.
50	oṉṟanu kūlam uraittāṉ. naṉṟē
	ūrivaṉ viṭuttatum pōrivaṇ toṭuttatum.
	eṇṇiya koḷkaik kicaiyum pukaṉṟavai. 
	naṇṇutum neruṅki. nallatu ! tirumpiṉaṉ. 
				(puruṭōttamaṉ tirumpivara)
puru:		(taṉimoḻi)
		eṉṟum kaṇṭilam iṉṟukaṇ ṭatupōl.
	ettaṉai mukattiṭait tattuṟu tuyaram!
	ivvayiṉ yāṉvan tiṟuttanāḷ mutalāk 
	kauvaiyiṉ āḻntaṉai pōlum! aiyō!
			(kuṭilaṉ etirvara) 
	(kuṭilaṉai nōkki) 
	jaṭiti! peyareṉ! cāṟṟuti! tatkṣaṇam!
kuṭi:		aṭiyēṉ! aṭiyēṉ! kuṭilaṉ! aṭimai!
puru:	60	vantateṉ iruḷvayiṉ? vāḷiṭeṉ aṭiyil!
kuṭi:		ventiṟal vēntaniṉ veṉṟikoḷ pācaṟai
	cērntuṉ amaiyam tērntu toḻutuōr
	vārttainiṉ tiruccevi cērttiṭak karuti
	vantaṉaṉ aṭiyēṉ: tantatu teyvam
65	uṉṟaṉ tiruvaṭi taricaṉam uṭaṉē!
	cintaiyep paṭiyō appaṭi eṉceyal!
puru:		cepputi viraivil. cepputi vantamai!
kuṭi:		oppilā vīra! eppuva ṉamuniṉ
	meyppukaḻ pōrttuḷa tataṉāl, ippuvi
70	nīvaru muṉamē niṉvacap paṭṭut
	tāvarum iṉpam taṭaiyaṟat tuyppap
	pākkiyam peṟṟilam paṇṭē eṉṟuṉi
	ēkkamuṟ ṟiruntamai yāṉeṭu nāḷāy
	aṟintuḷaṉ. iṉṟunī āṟṟiya pōriṟ
75	ceṟintiru paṭaiyum cērtaru muṉamē
	muṟintiyām ōṭiya muṟaimaiyum cintaiyiṟ
	kaḷippaṭai yāmalē kaikalan tamaiyum
	veḷippaṭai yaṉṟō? vēnta!ip puviyōr
	velliṭa mumvelā iṭamum yāvum
80	nallavā ṟaṟivar. nāyiṉēṉ colvateṉ?
	vēcaiyar taṅkaḷ ācaiyil muyakkam
	aṉṟō iṉṟavar āṟṟiya pōrmuṟai?
	eṉcey vāravar? eṉceyvār? ēḻaikaḷ!
	niṉpukaḻ mayakkā maṉpatai ulakam
85	yāṇṭum iṉṟeṉil, aṇitām intap
	pāṇṭiyum niṉpāṟ pakaikoḷat takumē!
	oruvā ṟaṟamē yāyiṉum, maruvāk
	koṟṟavar piḻaikkāk kuṟṟamil mākkaḷai
	maṟṟavar maṉanilai muṟṟa aṟintapiṉ
90	karuṇaiyō kāytal, tarumanal luruvē!
puru:		(taṉatuḷ)
		yātō cūtoṉ ṟeṇṇiṉaṉ. aṟikuvam.
		(kuṭilaṉai nōkki)
		vēṇṭiya teṉṉai ataṉāl? viḷamputi.
kuṭi:		āṇṭakai yaṟiyā tatuveṉ? iṉṟu
	māṇṭavar pōka mīṇṭava rēṉum
95	māḷā vaḻinī āḷāy eṉṉak
	kaikuvip patēyalāṟ ceyvakai yaṟiyā
	aṭiyēṉ eṉcola! ā! ā! viṭiyil
	vāḷā māḷum maṉitar tokuti
	eṇṇi eṇṇi erikiṟa teṉṉuḷam.
100	eṇṇuti karuṇai! ivarkkuḷ tāykkoru
	putalvarāy vanta porunaret taṉaiyō?
	vatuvai muṟṟuṟā vayavaret taṉaiyō?
	putumaṇam purinta puruṭaret taṉaiyō?
	nonta cūliṉar nōvu pārātu
105	vantivaṇ aṭainta maḷḷaret taṉaiyō?
	tāymukam varuntal kaṇṭaḻun taṉciṟu
	cēymukam maṟavāc cerunaret taṉaiyō?
	ceyiruṟa muḻantāḷ cērntaḻu pālarait
	tuyiliṭait tuṟanta cūraret taṉaiyō?
puru:      	110	cari, cari! ivaiyuṉ aracark kāṅku
	cāṟṟā toḻintateṉ?
kuṭi:					cāṟṟileṉ?
	pōṟṟāṉ yārcolum puntiyum caṟṟum.
	aṉpilaṉ; piṟarpaṭum tuṉpam ciṟitum
	aṟiyā veṟiyaṉ. aṉpo ṭimmālai
115	kuṟiyā nīviṭu tūtaiyum koṇṭilaṉ.
	aṇṭiya cīva rācikaḷ aṉaittaiyum
	maṇṭamar itilniṉ vaivāḷ taṉakkē
	iraiyiṭal oṉṟē viratamāk koṇṭaṉaṉ.
	pittaṉ oruvaṉ taṉṉāl ittamiḻ
120	nāṭelām veṟuñcuṭu kāṭāy viṭumē.
	āva! ipperum pāvamum paḻiyum
	añciṉēṉ; añciṉēṉ! eñcalil karuṇai
	yuruvē! aṭiyēṟ korumoḻi taruvaiyēl
	oruvark kēṉu muṟutuya riṉṟi
125	aracaṉum puricaiyum arainoṭip pōtiluṉ
	karatala māmoru kaucalam,
	kāṭṭukē ṉaṭiyēṉ kēṭṭaru ḷaracē!
(nilaimaṇṭila āciriyappā)
puru:	(taṉatuḷ)	pātakā! vicuvāca kātakā!
				ā! hā!
					(cirittu)

130	uraitava ṟātuṉ kuṭaikkī ḻotuṅkuvar.
	maṅkala maturaiyu miṅkivar vaḻiyē
	uṉṉā ṇaikkī ḻo tuṅkutal tiṇṇam.
	tolpuvi tōṟṟiyatu toṭṭara curimai
	malkiya puviyiḵ tataṉāl, “maṉṉavaṉ”
135	eṉṟapē roṉṟunī yīvaiyē leṉṟum,
	niṉṉā ṇaiyiṉkīḻ niṉṟunī muṉṉar
	vēṇṭiya tāroṭu nīrumē yaṉṟimaṟ
	ṟīṇṭuḷa evaiyē yāyiṉum vēṇṭiṭil,
	ciramēṟ cumantuṉ muracā raṉantaik
140	kōyil vāyiliṟ koṇarntuṉ tiruvaṭi
	kaṇṭumīḷ vatuvē katiyaṭi yēṟkām.
	paṇṭirā kavaṉṟaṉ paḻampakai ceṟṟu
	veṉṟatō rilaṅkai vipīṣaṇaṉ kāttavā
	ṟiṉṟunī veṉṟanā ṭiṉitukāt tiṭuvēṉ.
puru:     	145	camarttaṉ mettavum! amaittatan tirameṉ?

	yāvaru maṟiyā mēvaruñ curuṅkai
	oṉṟuḷatu. avvaḻi ceṉṟiṭi lakkaṇaṅ
	kaitavaṉ kaitiyā yeytuva ṉuṉṉaṭi.

		(cēvakarai nōkki)	yāratu?

	meymmai yalātevar viḷampuvar?
					(aruḷvarataṉ vara)

puru:		kaittaḷai kāṟṟaḷai koṭuvā noṭiyil.

kuṭi:		aṇitē! aḵtō! caraṇam pukunta
155	eḷiyēṟ kapaya miyamputi yiṟaiva!
puru:		avvaḻi yōnī yaṇaintaṉai?
kuṭi:		ām! ām!
	cevviti ṉorumoḻi ceppiṭi luṭaṉē
	kāṭṭuva ṉaṭiyēṉ.
		(aruḷvarataṉuñ cēvakarum vilaṅku koṇṭuvara)

kuṭi:		aiyō! aiyō! ōhō! ceytateṉ?
160	meyyē muṟṟum. poyyilai! poyyilai!
		(aruḷvarataṉ vilaṅku pūṭṭa)
puru:		etticai yuḷatac curuṅkai? ēkāy!
	cittira vataiyē ceyvēṉ piḻaippil!
kuṭi:		(aḻutu)
		tēṭiyē vantu ceppiya vaṭiyēṉ
	ōṭiyō pōvēṉ? ōhō! uṟuti
165	muntiyē tantiṭil...
puru:		mūṭuniṉ pāḻvāy!
	cēraṉ vijayamun tiruṭāṉ! aṟikuti.
		(cēvakarai nōkki)
		cūrar patiṉmar cūḻuka virupuṟam!
		(kuṭilaṉai nōkki)

	ettiṟam piḻaippiṉuñ cittira vataiyē!
		(yāvarum curuṅkai nōkkip pōka)

aintām aṅkam: mutaṟ kaḷam muṟṟiṟṟu.
iraṇṭām  kaḷam
	iṭam	:	kaṉṉimāṭat torucār.
	kālam	:	yāmam.
(cila tōḻippeṇkaḷum oru kiḻaviyum aḷavaḷāvi irukka)
(nilaimaṇṭila āciriyappā)
kiḻavi:		etukkumiv viḷakkum icciṟu cempum
	otukkivai ammā! utavum vaḻiyil.
mutaṟṟōḻi:	eṉṉaṭi kiḻavi coṉṉāl aṟikilai.
	pōmvaḻi aṟiyōm! pōmiṭam aṟiyōm!
 5	mantirak kuḷikaiyō! antara mārkkamō!
	mūṭṭaiyēṉ? muṭiccēṉ? kēṭṭiyō tōḻi?
	kātu millai! kaṇṇu millai!
	ētumillai! ēṉuyir iruppatō!
kiḻavi:		kiḻavipēc cēṟkumō kiṉṉarak kārikku!
10	paṭumpō taṟivai!ip paṭiyē paṇṭu
	muṉṉoru caṇṭaiyil uṉṉaip peṟumuṉ
ōṭiṉōm...

		(kiḻavi pōka)
		cirikkavā? eṉceya! civaṉē! civaṉē!
			(nakaikka)
2-m tōḻi:	ammaṇi eṉceytāḷ? akkāḷ! ataṉpiṉ
15	‘appaṭi aracaṉ mīṇṭāṉ.’ ceppāy!
mutaṟṟōḻi:	eppaṭic ceppayāṉ? ēntiḻai paṭṭapāṭu.
	ayyō! attuyar teyvamē aṟiyum!
	maṉṉavaṉ vācal kaṭantāṉ eṉumuṉam
	taṉṉilai taḷarntāḷ, cāyntāḷ. vāṇiyum
20	arukuḷa ceviliyum yāṉumāy viraivil 
	tāṅkiṉōm pāṅkuḷa amaḷiyiṟ cērttōm.
	mūccilai; pēccilai; mukamelām veyarvai.
	iṭṭakai iṭṭakāl iṭṭavap paṭiyē.
	ippaṭi muṭintatē! iṉiyeṉ ceyvōm!
25	tappumō ivvoru tattumeṉ ṟeṇṇi
	ēṅkiṉōm, tiyaṅkiṉōm; pāṅkirun taḻutōm.
2-m tōḻi:	aiyō teyvamē! appō tavaḷuyir
	paṭṭapā ṭetuvō!kaṭṭam! kaṭṭam!
mutaṟṟōḻi:	vitiyitu! alatitu kataiyilum uḷatō?
30	nontapuṇ ataṉilē vantiṭum nūṟiṭi.
	tantai tēṟiṭat taṉtuyar maṟaittu
	makiḻcci kāṭṭiṉāṉ. vantatit taḷarcci.
	mūṭiṭil tīyum mūḷumum maṭaṅkāy.
2-m tōḻi:	ettaṉai vētaṉai! ettaṉai cōtaṉai!
35	yātu maṟiyāṭ kētit tuṇipu?
	ōtiya kaṭṭurai orumuṟai iṉiyum
	naviluti akkāḷ!
mutaṟṟōḻi:			naṅkainaṉ moḻiyeṉ 
	ceviyiṭai iṉiyum maṇipōl tikaḻum! 
	aracaṉai aṭipaṇin torucār otuṅki 
40	nīkkamil aṉpum ūkkamum kaḷippum
	kāṭṭiya matimukam kōṭṭiyē niṉṟa 
	tōṟṟameṉ kaṇṇiṉ māṟṟutal aritē!
	“eṉṉō itaṟkum yōcaṉai entāy!
	koṉṉē varuntalai! koḷkaiyiṟ piṟaḻā 
45	nītinam pāleṉil nērvatu jayamē.
	ētataṟ kaiyam? ituviṭ ṭaṭimai
	peyarvatu peritala, pērula kataṟkut
	tuyarvarum ellainam tuyarnōk kutalō
	perumai! aṇṇitē muṉiyiṭam; karutiya 
50	pirivō orutiṉam! kuruvum tantaiyum
	camameṉiṟ cuntara vimalaṉ taṉtirup
	pātā taravē pōtā tōtuṇai?
	āyiṉum attaṉai avacikam āyiṉ,
	ākuka āññaip paṭiyē! taṭaiyilai.
55	aṉṉaiyum niṉṉai aṉṟivē ṟaṟiyēṉ.
	uṉṉatē ivvuṭal. uṉtiru uḷḷam
	uṉṉiya paṭiyelām uvappac ceykuvaṉ.
	aṭimaiyiṉ kavalaiyāl aracark kiyalpām
	kaṭamaiyiṟ piṟaḻum kalakkam vilakkuvai!
60	aṉpām uṉpāl aiya! uṉmakaḷ
	vēṇṭum varamelām yāṇṭumiv voṉṟē.”
2-m tōḻi:	moḻiyō ituvum? āā! āā!
	ituvelām kāṇavō eḻutiṉaṉ piramaṉ?
mutaṟṟōḻi:	etuvelām kāṇavō iruppatu ikkaṇ?
2-m tōḻi:	eṉcey kiṉṟaṉaḷ ippōtu ēḻai?
mutaṟṟōḻi:	vañciyiv aṟaiyē varuvaḷ vallai!
	ētō eḻutu kiṉṟaṉaḷ. vāṇi
	kōtiniṉ ṟāṟṟu kiṉṟaṉaḷ kūntal.
2-m tōḻi:	nīrā ṭiṉaḷō innici?
mutaṟṟōḻi: 	ām!ām!
70	eḻuntu vācanī rāṭi muṉcurat
	taḻuntiya aṉṟutāṉ aṇintaveṇ paṭṭiṉaik
	koṭuvarap paṇittaṅ katuvē tarittu

	aṉṟirā aṇintamut tāramum aṇintu,
75	niṉṟutaṉ nilaiyelām āṭiyil nōkki,

	aṉṟupōl aṉṟō iṉṟeṉ nilaimai!’
	eṉṟu ciṟumuṟu valittaṉaḷ. eṉcola!

110	tītaṟa iṉpam tuyppanī eṇṇil

115	maṉakkaḷaṅ kaṅkaḷām mācukaḷ aṉaittum

155	iṇaiyilā uṉṉaṭik kiṉṟupōl eṉṟum

160	ētitu vāṇi! eṉ maṇam taṉakkō

5	keṭuttāṉ kiḻavaṉ.

	teriyīr pōlum!

		vāṇiyiṉ maṉappaṭi maṉṟal naṭattalum
	ivvaram iraṇṭum ammaṇi vēṇṭa
10	aḷittaṉaṉ aṉumati kaḷippuṭaṉ aracaṉ.
mutaṟpaṭai:	irutirai iṭṭavā ṟippō taṟintēṉ.
	orutirai vāṇikku orutirai maṇikkē.
3-m paṭai:		ettirai tāykkeṉ ṟiyamputi. kēṭpōm.
mutaṟpaṭai:	ittirai tāykkām.
3-m paṭai:				cī! cī! attirai.
2-m paṭai:	ettirai āyiṉeṉ? ēṉirai kiṉṟīr?
mutaṟpaṭai:	irutirai vantavā ṟituvē āyiṉum
	orutiraik korutirai ettaṉai tūram?
3-m paṭai:		atō avaṉ aṟikuvaṉ. aṟintitō varuvēṉ
		(3-m paṭaiñaṉ maṟṟōriṭam pōka)
mutaṟpaṭai:	aiyō! poyyaṟum aṉṉaiyam maṇikkum
20	poyyaṉ palatē vaṉukkumō poruttam?
2-m paṭai:		varuttamēṉ uṉakku? maṉṉaṉ tiruvuḷak
	karuttaṉu carittunām kāṭṭalē kaṭamai
(3-m paṭaiñaṉ mīṇṭum vara)
3-m paṭai:		(mutaṟpaṭaiñaṉai nōkki)
		ippuṟam varuti. ceppuvaṉ rakaciyam.
	cattiyam ceytapiṉ cāṟṟiṉaṉ. nīyum
25	ettiṟat tōrkkum iyampalai. pattiram!
	attirai maṇattiṟ kaṉṟu. maṟ ṟappuṟam
	neruṅkiya curuṅkaiyoṉ ṟuḷatām. avvaḻi
	cellil vekutolai celumām. ippōr
	vellum varaiyum avvaḻi maṇantōr
30	iruvarum eytivāḻn tirupparām.
mutaṟpaṭai:				cari! cari!
	poruvarum puttimāṉ kuṭilaṉ. ettaṉai
	viraiviṉiṟ camaittāṉ! vekutiṟam uṭaiyāṉ.
3-m paṭai:		ippō taṉṟatu; nakarā rampam
	eppōtu appō tēvarum tuyaram
35	karutimuṉ ceytaṉaṉ.
mutaṟpaṭai:		oruvarum aṟintilam!
			(murukaṉ vā)
3-m paṭai:		yāratu, murukaṉō? nāraṇaṉ eṅkē?
murukaṉ:		nāraṇaṉ appuṟam pōyiṉāṉ; varuvaṉ.
3-m paṭai:		piḻaittīr immuṟai.
muru:		piḻaittilam eṉṟum!
3-m paṭai:		attiraic ceyti aṟivāy. vaittatār?
muru:      	40	vaittatu ārāyiṉeṉ? ventatu vīṭu!
(iruvarum nakaikka)
2-m paṭai:	vāyiṉai mūṭumiṉ. vantaṉaṉ maṇamakaṉ.
muru:		īyō vāyil ēṟiṭa nāyē!
mutaṟpaṭai:	aracaṉum muṉivarum atōvaru kiṉṟar!
		(jīvakaṉ, cuntaramuṉivar, karuṇākarar, niṣṭāparar,
	palatēvaṉ, naṭarācaṉ, nārāyaṇaṉ mutaliyōr vara)
jīva:		irumiṉ irumiṉ! namarkāḷ yārum!
		(jīvakaṉ, muṉivar mutaliyōr tattam iṭattirukka)
	45	koluvō kollitu! maṇavaṟai! irumiṉ.
	palatēva rēnum pitāvitu kāṟum
	vantilar eṉṉai?
palatē:				maṉṉavar maṉṉa!
	antiyiṟ kaṇṭēṉ aṭiyēṉ. ataṉpiṉ
	oruvarum kaṇṭilar. taṉipō yiṉarām.
jīva:      	50	irumirum nīrum. eṅkē kiṉumnam
	kāriya mēyavar karuttep poḻutum.
		(nārāyaṇaṉai nōkki)
		pārīr avarpaṭum pāṭu.
nārā:		pārppēṉ!
	cattiyam cayikkumēṟ cāṟṟiya paṭiyē!
jīva:		ittakai uḻaippōr eppuva ṉamumilai. 
55	eṇṇinic cayitta ittoḻil iṉiyām
	paṇṇaṟ keṉtaṭai? cuvāmi! aṭikaḷ
	tantanaṉ mukūrttam vantatō?
cun:		vantatu.
		(puruṭōttamaṉum, kuṭilaṉum aruḷvarataṉ mutaliya

puru:		(kaṟpaṭaiyil aruḷvarataṉai nōkki)

	eṉpiṉ iruvar varuka.
		(taṉatuḷ)			ituveṉ? 
60	innici ettaṉai viḷakku! ētō! 
	maṉṉavai pōlum! mantirā lōcaṉai!
	ivarcun tararē! avarnaṭa rājar!
	ivarkaḷiṅ kuḷarō! eytiya tevvaḻi?
	ittirai etaṟkō? attirai etaṟkō?
65	ittaṉai kōlā kalameṉ capaikku?
	mālaiyum kōlamum kāṇiṉ maṇavaṟai
	pōlām. aṟintiṉip pōvatē naṉmai.
	mantiram āyiṉ maṟṟatum aṟivōm.
	intanal tirainamak kettaṉai utavi!
(tiraikkup piṉ maṟaintu niṟka)
jīva: 	70	eṉkulam kākka eṉavaruḷ paḻuttuk
	kaṅkaṇam kaṭṭiya karuṇā nitikāḷ! 
	maṉattiṟan tāḻnta matiman tirikāḷ!
	eṉakkeṉa uyirvāḻ eṉpaṭai vīrarkāḷ!
	orumoḻi kūṟiṭa aṉumati tarumiṉ.
75	āṭaiyiṉ ciṟappelām aṇivōr ciṟappē;
	pāṭaiyiṉ ciṟappelām payilvōr ciṟappē;
	eḷḷarum matikulac ciṟappelām, emarkāḷ!
	kaḷḷamil nummuṉōr kāppiṉ ciṟappē.
	ātalil umakkupa cāramyāṉ ōtutal.
80	meykkuyir kaikkunā viḷamputal māṉum.
	īṇṭukāt tiṭuvalyāṉ eṉakkaṭaṉ pūṇṭatum
	matikula maruntāy vāyttaeṉ ciṟumi
	vitaipaṭum  āleṉa viḷaṅkiṉaḷ. avaḷaik
	kāttiṭum upāyam kaṇṭiṭa iccapai 
85	cērttaṉaṉ eṉpatu terivīr nīvīr.
	iṉṟunām paṭṭatōr iḻukkiv vaikaṟai
	poṉṟiyō veṉṟō pōkkuvam tiṇṇam.
	orukulat toruvaṉ orumarat tōrilai.
	appaṭi aṉṟunam kaṟpakac ciṟukaṉi!
90	tappiṭiṉ matikulap peyarē tavaṟum.
	ariyavic cantiyaip peritum karutumiṉ.
	iruntiṭac ciṟiyaḷ: apāyam! taṉiyē
	pirintiṭap periyaḷ: piḻai!aḵ taṉṟiyum
	kulamuṭi veṇṇik kulaiyunam uḷattiṟ
95	kilaiyata ṉālōr iyalcamā tāṉam.
	ātalil ariyataṟ kālat tiyalpai
	yāteṉa nīvir āyntiyāṉ ippō
	tōtiṭum upāyat tāluṟu naṉmaiyum
	tīmaiyum naṉṟāyt terintu ceppumiṉ!
100	kuṭilaṉai aṟiyār yārik koṟṟavai?
2-m paṭai: 	kuṭilaṉai aṟiyumē kuvalayam aṉaittum.
jīva:		aṟintiṭil iṟumpū taṇaiyār yāvar?
	matiyuḷār yāravaṉ matiyati cayittiṭār?
	neñcuḷār yāravaṉ vaṉtiṟaṟ kañcār?
105	yārvaiyār avaṉiṭat tārā ārvam?
	uṇmaik kuṟaiviṭam; tiṇmaik kaṇikalam.
	cattiya vittu: pattiyuṉ mattaṉ.
	āḷviṉai taṉakkāḷ; kēḷvitaṉ kēḷvaṉ.
	ēṉmika? namarkāḷ? innaṭu niciyilum
110	yāṉaṟi yātuḻaik kiṉṟaṉaṉ eṉakkā.
	naṉṟē iṅkavaṉ ilāmaiyum : aṉṟēl
	taṟpukaḻ kēṭka aṟpamum icaiyāṉ.
puru:		(taṉatuḷ)
		ettaṉai kaḷaṅkamil cuttaṉ! kaṭṭam!
jīva:		paṟpala pākkiyam paṭaittuḷar paṇṭuḷōr.
115	opparum amaiccaṉai ippaṭi oruvarum
	muṉṉuḷōr peṟṟilar; piṉṉuḷār peṟuvatum
	aiyameṉ ṟuraippēṉ. aṉṉavaṉ putalvaṉ
	meymmaiyum, vāramum vīravā cāramum,
	patticēr puttiyum, yutticēr ūkkamum
120	uṭaiyaṉāy aṭaiyavum taṟpira timaipōl,
	iṉiyoru talaimuṟai taṉicē vakañceya
	iṅkuvīṟ ṟiruntilaṉ āyiṉ, emarkāḷ!
	eṅkunīr kaṇṭuḷīr icciṟu vayatiṟ
	palatē vaṉaippōṟ palitamām ciṟutaru?
2-m paṭai:125	ilaiyilai eṅkum! ivarpōl yāvar!
jīva:		eṉatara curimaiyum eṉatara ciyalpum
	tamatār uyirpōl tāmniṉait tituvarai
	evvaḷa vuḻaittuḷār ivviru varumeṉac
	cevvitiṉ eṉaiviṭa nīvirē terivīr.
130	ikkulam avarkku mikkatōr kaṭaṉpā
	ṭuṭaiyateṉ ṟoruvarum ayirppuṟār. ataṉāl
	taṭaiyaṟa akkaṭaṉ tavirkkavum nammuḷam
	kalakkiṭum apāyam vilakkavum orumaṇam
	eṇṇiṉēṉ. paṇṇuvēṉ icaivēl 							numakkum.
135	maṇaviṉai muṭinta maṟukaṇam maṇantōr
	iruvarum ivviṭam viṭuttunam muṉivarar
	tāpatam ceṉṟu taṅkuvar. ittakai
	āpatam karutiyē aruṭkaṭal aṭikaḷ
	tāmē varuntic camaittuḷār avviṭam
140	pōmā ṟoruciṟu puraiyaṟu curuṅkai.
	avvuḻi iruvarum aṭaintapiṉ, nammaik
	kavviya kauvaiyum kavalaiyum viṭutalāl,
	vañciyaṉ oruvaṉō, eñcalil ulakelām
	cēriṉum nammuṉ tīcceṟi pañcē.
145	ituvē eṉṉuḷam. ituvē namatu
	matikulam piḻaikkum mārkkameṉ ṟaṭikaḷum
	aruḷiṉar āññai. āyiṉum numatu
	teruḷuṟu cūḻcciyum terintiṭa viruppē.	1
(nēricai āciriyappā)
		uraiyīr cakaṭarē umatapip pirāyam.
cakaṭaṉ:  150	aracar kulamaṉṟu. āyiṉeṉ? cari cari!

		marukaṉ tappiya varuttam pōlum.

	175	maṟṟivar kavalai māṟṟiṭa orupā
	caṟṟicait tiṭuvaḷō vāṇi? cāṟṟuti!
vāṇi: 		(pāṭa)
(koccakak kalippā)
nīrnilaiyiṉ mutalaiyiṉvāy nilaikulainta orukarimuṉ
ōr muṟaiyuṉ peyar viḷikka utaviṉaivan teṉavuraippar;
ārtuyara aḷakkarviḻum aṟiviliyāṉ aḻaippataṟkuṉ
pērteriyēṉ āyiṭiṉum piṟakiṭalniṉ peruntakaiyō.	1
pāraracar tukiluriyap paritavikkum oruterivai
cīrtuvarai nakarkarutic citaivoḻintāḷ eṉauraippar;
ārtuṇaiyum aṟavirukkum aṟiviliyāṉ aḻaippataṟkuṉ
ūrteriyēṉ āyiṭiṉum uṟutitaral uṉakkurittē.	2
maṟalivara maṉampataṟum mārkkaṇṭaṉ uṉatiliṅkak
kuṟitaḻuvi aḻivilvaram koṇṭāṉmuṉ eṉavuraippar;
veṟikaḻumip poṟiyaḻiyum vempāvi viravutaṟkuṉ
neṟiyaṟiyēṉ āyiṭiṉum nērniṟṟal niṉataruḷē.	3
(āciriyappāviṉ toṭarcci)
cun:		etuvō itaṉiṉum ēṟpuṭaip pirārttaṉai?
	mantiram tantiram vaḻaṅkum naṟcepam
	yāvaiyum ituvē. pāvāy! maṉōṉmaṇī!
180	varuti ippuṟam. vāṅkuti mālai.
		(maṉōṉmaṇi maṇamālaikoṇṭu 
palatēvaṉetir vara)
		orutaṉi mutalvaṉ uṇarvaṉ uṉṉuḷam.
	uṉṉaṉ puṇmaiyēl iṉṉamum kāppaṉ.
	(puruṭōttamaṉ tiraiviṭṭu veḷivantu niṟka)
mutaṟpaṭai:	āṟṟēṉ! āṟṟēṉ! aiya! it tōṟṟam.
3-m paṭai:		ūṟṟirun toḻuki uḷvaṟan tatukaṇ.
4-m paṭai:		amaiyā nōkkamum imaiyā nāṭṭamum,
	ēṅkiya mukamum nīṅkiya itaḻum,
	uyirilā nilaiyum uṇarvilā naṭaiyum
	pārttiṭiṟ cūttirap pāvaiyē. pāvam!
	(maṉōṉmaṇi puruṭōttamaṉaik kāṇa: 
uṭaṉ avaṉ niṟkumiṭamē viraivil naṭakka)
yāva:		eṅkē pōkiṟāḷ? ituyār? ituyār?
puru:     	190 	iṅkō nīyuḷai! eṉṉuyir amirtē!
	(puruṭōttamaṉ talaitāḻkka: maṉōṉmaṇi mālai cūṭṭi avaṉ ṟōḷōṭu taḷarntu mūrccikka)
cun:		maṅkalam! maṅkalam! maṅkalam! umakkē!

palatē:  	195	koṉmiṉ! koṉmiṉ!
		(yāvarum puruṭōttamaṉaic cūḻa: 
		cuntarar kūṭṭam vilakka.)
cun:			niṉmiṉ! niṉmiṉ!
		(aruḷvarataṉum meykāppāḷarum vara)
aruḷvarataṉ:
	aṭaiyiṉ aṭaivīr yamapuram. akaṉmiṉ!
		(puruṭōttamaṉaiyum maṉōṉmaṇiyaiyuñ cūḻntu
	niṉṟu kākka)
yāva:		paṭaiyuṭaṉ pātakaṉ!			(piṉṉiṭa)
aruḷ:		(vilaṅkuṭaṉ kuṭilaṉaik kāṭṭi)
		pātakaṉ īṅkuḷāṉ.
jīva:		kuṭilā uṉakkumik keṭutiyēṉ? aiyō!
	aṭikāḷ! ituveṉ! ituveṉ! anīti!
200	aṟiyēṉ iccū taṟiyēṉ! aṟiyēṉ!
cun:		poṟu! poṟu! jīvaka! aṟikutum viraivil.
puru:		vañciyāṉ vañciyāṉ! maṉṉava! uṉcol
	añciṉēṉ. cūtuṉ amaiccaṉ ceykai.
	curuṅkaiyiṉ taṉmai colli yeṉṉaiyiṅ
205	koruṅkē aḻaittāṉ uṉṉakar kavara.
	uṉṉara curimaiyum uṉṉakar nāṭum
	eṉṉiṭam irantāṉ iccū titaṟkā!
	ōtiya curuṅkaiyiṉ uṇmaikaṇ ṭivaṉtaṉ
	cūtum turōkamum coliuṉait teruṭṭa
210	eṇṇiyāṉ vantuḻi ivvoḷi viḷakkum
	paṇṇiyal pāṭṭum paḻaiyapuṇ ṇiyamum
	tūṇṭiṭa īṇṭumaṟ ṟaṭaiyavum, yāṇṭum
	eṉatuyir avāviya ivvaru maruntai
	naṉaviṉiṟ kāṇavum naṇṇavum peṟṟēṉ.
215	pirikilam iṉimēl. uriyaniṉ urimai
	yātē āyiṉum ākuka. ītō!
	mīḷkuvaṉ. viṭaikoṭu nāḷaiyum
	vēṭpaiyēṟ kāṇpōm ñāṭpiṭai nāṭpē.
jīva:		uṇmaiyō? kuṭilā! uraiyāy!
	(kuṭilaṉ mukaṅ kaviḻttu niṟka)
nārā:						ituvuniṉ
220	uṇmaiyō! mauṉamēṉ?
yāva:					ōkō! pāvi!
nārā:		paṭapaṭat tiṭuniṉ pāḻvāy tiṟavāy!
cun:		viṭuviṭu! vicāraṇaik kituvaṉ ṟamaiyam!
	naṉmaiyē yāvum naṉmaiyāy muṭiyiṉ.
	vārāy jīvaka! pārāy uṉmakaḷ
225	tārāt taṉṉiru kaitōḷ cūṭṭi
	eṇpaṭu mārpiṭaik kaṇpaṭu nilaimai.
	irumaṉam ēṉiṉi; eṉṟumip paṭiyē
	marukaṉu makaḷum vāḻka! vāḻt tutiyē.
jīva:		kaṇmaṇī! ataṟkuṭ kaṇvaḷarn taṉaiyō!
230	uṉṉaiyum maṟantuṟaṅ kutiyēl iṉimēl
	eṉṉaiyeṅ keṇṇuvai? iṟumpū tiruvarum
	oruvarai oruvar uṇarntamai!
	(maṉōṉmaṇi tiṭukkiṭṭu viḻikka)
		veruvalai! maṇiyē! piriyīr iṉiyē!	3
	paḷḷa uvarkkaṭaliṟ pāyntōṭum veḷḷameṉa
	uḷḷam uvantōṭi oṉṟāṉāy--viḷḷā
	maṇiyiṉa toḷiyum malaratu maṇamum
	aṇipeṟu moḻiyiṉ aruttamum pōla,
	annici yākaveñ ñāṉṟum
	maṉṉiya aṉpuṭaṉ vāḻmati ciṟantē!
			(yāvarum vāḻtta)
aintām aṅkam: mūṉṟām kaḷam muṟṟiṟṟu.

(kalittuṟai)
ciṟitā yiṉumpaṟ ṟilātukai yaṟṟa tirumakaṭaṉ
kuṟiyān talaivaṉ kuṭilaṉpiṉ eytiya koḷkaikaṇṭīr
aṟivōm eṉunam akaṅkaram āṟum avattaiyiṉiṟ
ceṟivā yirukkun tirukku veḷippaṭum cīrituvē.

aintām aṅkam muṟṟiṟṟu.

maṉōṉmaṇīyam muṟṟiṟṟu.

purāṇakkatai viḷakkam
akattiyar kaṭalkuṭitta katai
pakkam 17
orukālattil mittirar varuṇar eṉpavarkaḷ pēraḻakiyākiya ūrvaciyaik kaṇṭu avaḷ mītu ācaikoṇṭārkaḷ. ataṉāl avarkaḷukku vintu veḷippaṭṭatu. oruvar vintu kuṭattilum, maṟṟoruvar vintu nīrilum viḻuntaṉa. kuṭattil viḻuntatiliruntu akattiyarum. nīril viḻuntatiliruntu vaciṣṭarum uṇṭāṉārkaḷ. kuṭattiliruntu uṇṭāṉapaṭiyiṉālē akattiyarukkuk kuṭamuṉi eṉṟu peyar ēṟpaṭṭatu.
tēvarkaḷukkum acurarkaḷukkum orukālattil pōr ēṟpaṭṭatu. viruttirācuraṉum ēṉaiya acurarkaḷum kaṭaliṉ uḷḷē pukuntu oḷintukoṇṭaṉar. appōtu intiraṉ akattiya muṉivariṭam vantu acurarkaḷai veḷippaṭuttavēṇṭum eṉṟu vēṇṭikkoḷḷa, ataṟku avar uṭaṉpaṭṭuk kaṭalnīraik kaiyiṉāl mukantu aruntiṉar. ataṉāl kaṭalnīr vaṟṟiyatu. appōtu aṅku maṟaintirunta virut tirācuraṉ  mutaliya acurarkaḷai intiraṉ koṉṟāṉ. piṟaku, intiraṉ vēṇṭikkoṇṭapaṭi akattiyar mīṇṭum nīraik kaṭalil umiḻntār.
	“	muṉikkaracu kaiyāl mukantu, muḻaṅkum
paṉikkaṭalum, uṇṇap paṭum.”
cakarar cākaram tōṇṭiya katai
parata kaṇṭattai aracāṇṭa cakara cakkaravartti oru kālattil acuva mēta yākam ceyyat toṭaṅkiṉār. yākam ceyvataṟku muṉṉar, yākak kutiraiyait tēcam eṅkum cuṟṟi valamvarumpaṭi aṉuppukiṟa vaḻakkampōla, tamatu kutiraiyai aṉuppiṉār. poṟāmai koṇṭa intiraṉ. oruvarum aṟiyāmal yākak kutiraiyaik koṇṭupōyp pātāḷa lōkattilē tavam ceytukoṇṭirunta kapila muṉivariṉ arukil kaṭṭiviṭṭuc ceṉṟāṉ. yākak kutirai ulakameṅkum tēṭiyum kāṇāmaiyāl, cakara cakkaravarttiyiṉ piḷḷaikaḷ aṟupati ṉāyiravarum pātāḷa lōkattiṟ ceṉṟu tēṭalām eṉṟu eṇṇi parata kaṇṭattiṉ kiḻakkup pakkattait tōṇṭikkoṇṭu pātāḷam ceṉṟārkaḷ. kutiraiyaik kaṇṭārkaḷ. ivarkaḷ tōṇṭiya perum paḷḷam nīr niṟaintu kaṭalāyiṟṟu. cakara cakkaravarttiyiṉ aṟupati ṉāyiram piḷḷaikaḷukkum cakarar (cakara cakkaravarttiyiṉ piḷḷaikaḷ) eṉpatu potuppeyar. cakarar tōṇṭiya paṭiyiṉāl kīḻkkaṭal cākaram eṉṟu peyarpeṟṟatu.
civaperumāṉ - nakkīrar katai
caṇpakamāṟaṉ eṉṉum pāṇṭiyaṉuṭaiya maṉaiviyiṉ kūntalil naṟumaṇam vīciṟṟu. pāṇṭiyaṉ, inta naṟumaṇam kūntalil iyaṟkaiyāka uṇṭāyiṟṟō. ceyaṟkaiyāka amaintatō eṉṟu aiyuṟṟāṉ. taṉatu aiyattait tīrppavarukku āyiram poṉ vekumati yaḷippatākak kūṟi, tamiḻc caṅka maṇṭapattil poṟkiḻiyait toṅka viṭṭāṉ.
appoḻutu ālavāyk kōvilil aruccaṉai ceyyum tarumi, maṇampuriyap paṇam illāmal iruntavaṉ civaperumāṉiṭam vēṇṭikkoḷḷa, avar ‘koṅkutēr vāḻkkai añciṟait tumpi’ eṉṟu toṭaṅkum oru ceyyuḷai iyaṟṟi ataṉaik koṇṭu pōyc caṅkattāriṭam paṭittukkāṭṭip poṟkiḻiyaip peṟṟuk koḷḷum paṭi kūṟiṉār. (kuṟuntokai 2-ām ceyyuḷaik kāṇka.)  tarumi avvāṟē caṅkattāriṭam ceṉṟu ac ceyyuḷaip paṭittuk kāṭṭip poṟkiḻiyaik kēṭṭāṉ. capaiyilirunta nakkīrar, “ic ceyyuḷil makaḷir kūntalukku iyaṟkai maṇam uṇṭu eṉṟu colliyiruppataṉāl itu kuṟṟamuḷḷa ceyyuḷ” eṉṟu ac ceyyuḷil kuṟṟam kaṇṭu kūṟiṉār.
ataik kēṭṭa tarumi mīṇṭum kōvilukkuc ceṉṟu civaperu māṉiṭam naṭantataik kūṟiṉāṉ. civaperumāṉ pulavar uruvattōṭu caṅka maṇṭapattiṟku vantār. vantu, “eṉ ceyyuḷil kuṟṟam kaṇṭavaṉ yār?” eṉṟu kēṭṭār. nakkīrar, tāṉ kuṟṟam kaṇṭatākak kūṟiṉār. “eṉṉa kuṟṟam?” eṉṟu civaṉ kēṭka, avar muṉpu kūṟiyatu pōla, “makaḷirkkuk kūntalil iyaṟkai maṇam uṇṭeṉpatu kuṟṟam” eṉṟu kūṟiṉār. civaperumāṉ ataṟku viṭai kūṟa muṭiyāmal, tamatu neṟṟik kaṇṇait tiṟantu kāṭṭiṉār. nakkīrar ataṟkum añcāmal,  “neṟṟik kaṇṇaik kāṭṭiṉālum kuṟṟam kuṟṟamē!” eṉṟār.
tiruñāṉacampantariṉ puṉalvātam
tiruñāṉacampantar maturaikkuc ceṉṟapōtu pāṇṭiyaṉ capaiyilē avarukkum camaṇarkaḷukkum camayavātam nikaḻntatu. atil puṉalvātamum oṉṟu. iru camayattārum taṅkaḷ matak karuttai eḻutiya paṉaiyēṭṭai vaikaiyāṟṟil viṭuvatu eṉṟum, yāruṭaiya ēṭu āṟṟukku etirākac ceṉṟu karaiyēṟukiṟatō avaruṭaiya camayamē meyc camayam eṉṟu oppukkoḷḷappaṭum eṉṟum muṭivu ceyyappaṭṭatu. avvāṟē tiruñāṉacampantarum camaṇarum tattam matak koḷkaiyiṉai eḻutiya paṉaiyēṭṭai vaikaiyāṟṟil viṭṭaṉar. camaṇar eḻuti viṭṭa ēṭu āṟṟu veḷḷattil aṭittuc ceṉṟatu. tiruñāṉacampantar viṭṭa ēṭu veḷḷattiṟku etirākac ceṉṟu karai otuṅkiṟṟu.
imayamum potikaiyum camamāṉa katai
pakkam17 
orukālattil civaperumāṉ malaiyaracaṉ makaḷākiya pārvatiyait  tirumaṇam ceytār. at tirumaṇam imayamalaiyil naṭaipeṟṟatu. at tirumaṇattaik kāṇat tēvarum maṉitarum ēṉai yōrum imayamalaikkuc ceṉṟaṉar. ataṉāl imayamalai kaṉatti ṉāl tāḻntu, potikaimalai uyarntatu. atukaṇṭa civaperumāṉ, akattiya muṉivaraip potikaimalaikkup pōkumpaṭi kūṟiṉār. ataṉ paṭiyē akattiyar vantu potikaimalaiyil taṅkiṉār. appōtu, paḷuvillātapaṭiyāl mēleḻunta potikai malai tāḻntu, imayamum potikaiyum camamāka niṉṟaṉa.
cannu kātil kaṅkai veḷippaṭṭa katai
pakkam 18
pakīrataṉ eṉpavaṉ neṭuṅkālam tavam iruntu ākāya kaṅkaiyaip pūlōkattil koṇṭu vantāṉ. pūmiyil vanta kaṅkai pāyntu ōṭiyatu. vaḻiyil cannu muṉivar yākam ceytu koṇṭirun tār.  avar, kaṅkaiyāṟu ōṭivaruvataik kaṇṭu. atu yāka kuṇṭattil pukuntu yākat tīyai aḻittuviṭum eṉṟu añci. kaṅkainīr muḻuvataiyum kuṭittuviṭṭār. appōtu pakīrataṉum, tēvarkaḷum muṉivarkaḷum kaṅkaiyāṟṟai veḷiyē viṭumpaṭi cannuvai vēṇṭikkoṇṭaṉar. ivarkaḷiṉ vēṇṭukōḷukku iṇaṅki cannu muṉivar. kaṅkaiyāṟṟait tamatu kātiṉ vaḻiyāka veḷiyē viṭṭār. itaṉāl, kaṅkai yāṟṟukkuc cānuvi eṉṉum peyar ēṟpaṭṭatu.
kākkai kāviriyai uṇṭākkiya katai
pakkam 18. vāyacam kaviḻtta poṉṉi
intiraṉ pūñcōlai amaikka virumpiṉāṉ. cōlaikku nīr vēṇṭumākaiyāl, avaṉ vināyakak kaṭavuḷai vaḻipaṭṭāṉ. avvamayam potikai malaiyai nōkkip pōykkoṇṭirunta akattiya muṉivar, kamaṇṭalattaik kīḻē vaittapōtu vināyakar kākkai vaṭivamākac ceṉṟu kamaṇṭala nīraik kaviḻttuviṭṭār. annīr perukik kāviriyāṟākap pāyntatu.
maṉmataṉai eritta katai 
pakkam 22
tārakācuraṉai aḻippataṟkāka murukakkaṭavuḷai utavavēṇṭum eṉṟu tēvarkaḷ civaperumāṉai vēṇṭiṉārkaḷ. appōtu civaperumāṉ yōkattil amarntiruntār. yōka niṣṭaiyaik kalaittup pārvati mītu iccai koḷḷumpaṭic ceyya tēvarkaḷ maṉmataṉai vēṇṭiṉārkaḷ. maṉmataṉ ceṉṟu malarkkaṇaikaḷaic civaperumāṉmītu eṟintāṉ. avar neṟṟik kaṇṇait tiṟantu pārttār. maṉmataṉ erintu cāmpalāṉāṉ. maṉmataṉ maṉaivi irati taṉ kaṇavaṉaip piḻaikkumpaṭi irantu vēṇṭiṉāḷ. civaperumāṉ, maṉmataṉai uyirppittu eḻuppi, iratikku maṭṭum teriyumpaṭiyum maṟṟavarkaḷukkuk kāṇappaṭāta paṭiyum amaittār. ākavē, maṉmataṉ aṉaṅkaṉ (uruvam illātavaṉ) eṉṟu peyar peṟṟāṉ. avaṉ aintuvitamāṉa pū ampukaḷai eytu ciṟṟiṉpa uṇarcciyai maṉitar uḷḷattil ūṭṭukiṟāṉ eṉpatu purāṇak katai.
kaṭuvuṇṭa kaṇṭar
pakkam 22
tēvarkaḷum acurarkaḷum pāṟkaṭalaik kaṭaintārkaḷ. appōtu ak kaṭaliliruntu cila poruḷkaḷ tōṉṟiṉa. avaṟṟuṭaṉ nañcum tōṉṟiyatu. nañcaik kaṇṭavuṭaṉ avarkaḷ añci ōṭiṉārkaḷ. civaperumāṉiṭam ceṉṟu pāṟkaṭalil nañcu veḷippaṭṭataik kūṟiṉārkaḷ. civaperumāṉ, ālāla cuntararai aṉuppi nañcaik koṇṭuvarac ceytu ataṉait tamatu vāyil iṭṭu viḻuṅkiṉār.  viḻuṅkumpōtu pārvatiyār, nañcu uḷḷē pōkātapaṭi avar kaḻuttil niṟuttiṉār. itaṉāl civaperumāṉukkuk kaṭuvuṇṭa kaṇṭāṉ (kaṭu - viṣam) eṉṟum, nañcuṇṭa kaṇṭaṉ eṉṟum, kāḷa kaṇṭaṉ, kaṟaik kaṇṭaṉ, nīlakaṇṭaṉ eṉṟum peyarkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭaṉa. 
ariccantiraṉ katai
pakkam 31
poy collā viratattōṭu vāḻnta ariccantiraṉaip poyyaṉākka vicuvāmittirar avaṉukkup pala tuṉpaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭac ceykiṟār.  ariccantiraṉiṭam perumporuḷai yākattiṉ poruṭṭu yācittup peṟṟukkoṇṭu, apporuḷai avaṉiṭamē vaittup pōkiṟār. piṟaku nāṭṭiyap peṇkaḷai aṉuppi aracaṉiṭamiruntu apporuḷkaḷait tāṉamākap peṟumpaṭi ceykiṟār. poruḷ illāta camayattil vantu kēṭṭu āṭciyaip peṟṟuk koḷkiṟār. ivvāṟu poruḷaiyum aracaiyum iḻanta ariccantiraṉ kaṭaiciyil taṉṉaiyum taṉ maṉaiviyaiyum makaṉaiyum viṟkumpaṭiyāṉa ikkaṭṭāṉa   nilaiyaṭaiyumpaṭi ceykiṟār. kaṭaiciyākat tuṉpaṅkaḷ nīṅkiṉār. cattiyamē vellum eṉpatai niṟuviṉār.
intiraṉ urukkaranta katai 
pakkam 31
ciyavaṉa muṉivar intiraṉukku avirppākam koṭukkāmal, acuviṉi tēvarkaḷukkuk koṭuppataik kaṇṭu intiraṉ am muṉivar mēlē vaccirāyutattai eṟintāṉ. avar avaṉaic capittār.  ataṉāl avaṉ kaṭalil oḷintāṉ eṉpatu oru katai. 
turuvāca muṉivar tēvēntiraṉukku  oru malaraik koṭukka avaṉ atai vāṅkit tāṉ ēṟiyirunta airāvatayāṉaiyiṉ talaiyil vaittāṉ. anta yāṉai antap pūvait tumpikkaiyiṉāl eṭuttut taṉ kālil vaittut tēyttatu. atukaṇṭu ciṉanta turuvāca muṉivar avaṉai vēṭaṉākumpaṭi capittār. intiraṉ ac cāpattiṉāl kāṭṭil vēṭaṉākat tirintāṉ eṉpatu maṟṟoru katai.
intiraṉ akalikaiyaik kaṟpaḻitta kāraṇattiṉāl, kaucika muṉivar avaṉ uṭampu muḻuvatum peṇkuṟiyākak kaṭavatu eṉṟu capikka, ac cāpam ēṟṟa intiraṉ veṭki veḷiyē varamuṭiyāmal maṟaintu vāḻntāṉ eṉpatu iṉṉoru katai.
kaṭalil amutam tōṉṟiya katai 
pakkam 97
tēvarkaḷum acurarkaḷum pāṟkaṭalaik kaṭaintārkaḷ. mēru malaiyai mattākavum vācuki eṉṉum pāmpai mattaik kaṭaiyum kayiṟākavum koṇṭu tēvarkaḷ orupuṟamum  acurarkaḷ orupuṟamum iruntu kaṭaintapōtu, ap pāṟkaṭaliliruntu amutam tōṉṟiyatu.  amutam eṭukkat tēvarkaḷōṭu uḻaitta acurarkaḷukku uriya pākattaik koṭukkāmal, tēvarkaḷ vañcaṉaiyāka atai eṭuttuk koṇṭu pōyt tāṅkaḷ maṭṭum aruntiṉārkaḷ.  amutattai aruntiyapaṭiyāl nōyiṉṟi neṭuṅkālam vāḻntārkaḷ.
 pāmpu cantiraṉai viḻuṅkiya katai 
pakkam 126
pāṟkaṭalaik kaṭaintapōtu atiliruntu amutam kiṭaittatu. anta amutattait tammuṭaṉ uḻaitta acurarkaḷukkup paṅku koṭukkāmal koṇṭupōyt tēvarkaḷ maṭṭum pakirntu cāppiṭ ṭārkaḷ. appōtu irāku kētu eṉṉum iraṇṭu acurarkaḷ tēvarkaḷ pōla uruvam koṇṭu  tēvarkaḷ amutam cāppiṭukiṟa pantiyil pōy uṭkārntārkaḷ. itaiyaṟinta cūriyaṉ cantiraṉākiya tēvarkaḷ. amutattaip paṅkiṭukiṟa viṣṇuviṉiṭam kuṟippākat terivittār kaḷ. viṣṇu avarkaḷai aṭittut turattiṉār.  ataṉāl, cūriya cantirarkaḷiṉmēl pakaikoṇṭu irāku kētukkaḷākiya acurarkaḷ pāmpiṉ uruvaṅ koṇṭu avarkaḷai viḻuṅkuvatākavum. appaṭi viḻuṅkuvataṉāltāṉ cūriya kirakaṇam, cantira kirakaṇam ēṟpaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟum purāṇak katai kūṟukiṟatu.
kajēntira mōkṣam 
pakkam 291
kajēntiraṉ eṉṉum yāṉai tirumāl mītu paktikoṇṭu, nāḷ tōṟum nīrōṭaiyil tāmaraippūvaip paṟittup perumāḷaip pūcittukkoṇṭiruntatu.  orunāḷ oru mutalai yāṉaiyiṉ kālaip piṭittu nīril iḻuttatu. yāṉaiyum palaṅkoṇṭa maṭṭum mutalaiyaik karaikku iḻuttatu. ataṉāl  muṭiyavillai. mutalai yāṉaiyai nīril iḻuttuc ceṉṟatu. avvamayam, yāṉai tirumālai niṉaittatu. tirumāl vantu tamatu kaiccakkarattiṉāl mutalaiyaik koṉṟu yāṉaiyai viṭuvittu, ataṟku mōkṣam tantaruḷiṉār. 
tukilurinta katai 
pakkam 291
pāṇṭavar aivarum kauravaruṭaṉ cūtāṭi nāṭu nakaram mutaliyavaikaḷait tōṟṟaṉar.  kaṭaiciyil, tarumaputtirar, tiraupati yaiyum paṇaiyam vaittuc cūtāṭiṉār. avaḷaiyum tōṟṟār. appōtu, turiyōtaṉaṉ tiraupatiyaic capaiyil aḻaittu avaḷ tukilai aviḻttu māṉapaṅkam ceytāṉ. tiraupatikku utavi ceyvār oruvarumilar. appōtu tiraupati, kaṇṇapirāṉait tiyāṉam ceytāḷ.  kaṇṇaṉ tiruvaruḷāl avaḷ aṇintirunta āṭai vaḷarntukoṇṭēyiruntatu. ivvāṟu kaṇṇapirāṉ tiruvaruḷiṉāl tiraupati māṉam kāppāṟṟappaṭṭāḷ.
mārkkaṇṭēyaṉ katai
pakkam 291
mārkkaṇṭēyaṉ eṉpavar naṟkuṇamum nalloḻukkamum uṭaiyavarāyiruntār. āṉāl, avarukku aṟpāyuḷ eṉpatu aṟintu avaruṭaiya peṟṟōrkaḷ maṉam kavaṉṟārkaḷ. mārkkaṇṭēyar, tamatu peṟṟōriṉ maṉa varuttattait tīrkkumporuṭṭuc civaliṅka vaḻipāṭu ceytukoṇṭiruntār. avarukkup patiṉāṟu vayatāṉa pōtu, avar uyiraik kavara iyamaṉ vantāṉ. mārkkaṇṭēyar tām pūcaiceytukoṇṭirunta civaliṅkattaik kaṭṭit taḻuvik koṇṭār. iyamaṉ pācakkayiṟṟai eṟintu avar uyiraik kavara muyaṟci ceytāṉ. appōtu civaperumāṉ veḷippaṭṭu yamaṉai utaittut taḷḷi mārkkaṇṭēyarukku eṉṟum patiṉāṟuvayatu tantaruḷiṉār eṉpatu purāṇak katai.
***


THE SECRET WAY
By The Rt. Hon. Lord Lytton
The very striking legend which suggests the following poem is found in Athenaeus, book xiii. c. 35. It is there given as a quotation from the History of Alexander, by Chares of Mitylene.’ The author adds, that ‘ the story is often told by the barbarians who dwell ia Asia, and is exceedingly admired; and they have painted representations of the story in their temples and palaces, and also in their private houses.’ In constructing the plot of the poem, I have? made some variations in incident and denouement from the meagre outlines of the old romance preserved in Athenaeus, with a view of heightening the interest which springs from the groundwork of the legend. I should add that the name of the Scythian king’s daughter is changed from Odatis, which, for narrative purpose, a little too nearly resembles that of her father, Omartes-to Argiope : a name more Hellenic it is true, but it may be reasonably doubted whether that of Odatis be more genuinely Scythian. For the sake of euphony, the name of the Persian Prince is softened from Zariadres to Zariades. This personage is said by the author whom Athenaeus quotes, to have been the brother of Hystaspes, and to have held dominion over the country from above the Caspian Gates to the river Tanais (the modern Don). Assuming that he existed historically, and was the brother of Hystaspes and uncle to Darius I, he would have held the dominions assigned to him, as a satrap under Cambyses, not as an independent sovereign. But in a romance of this kind, it would be hypercritical, indeed, to demand strict historical accuracy. Although . the hero of the legend would have been, as described, of purely Persian origin (a royal Achaemenian), and the people subjected to him would not have belonged to Media proper, in the poem he is sometimes called the Mede, and his people Medes, according to an usage sufficiently common among Greek writers when speaking generally of the rulers and people of the great Persian Empire. It may scarcely be worth while to observe that though in subsequent tales where the Hellenic deities are more or less prominently introduced or referred to, their Hellenic names are assigned to them, yet in the passing allusions made in this poem to the God of War or the Goddess of Morning, it was judged more agreeable to the general reader to designate those deities by the familiar names of Mars and Aurora, rather than by the Greek appellations of Ares and Eos.
MARTES, King of the wide plains which, north , 
Of Tanais, pasture steeds for Scythian Mars, 
	Forsook the simple ways
		And Nomad tents of his unconquered fathers;	4
And in the fashion of the neighbouring Medes,
Built a great city girt with moat and wall, 
	And in the midst thereof
		A regal palace dwarfing piles in Susa	8
With vast foundations rooted into earth,
And crested summits soaring into Heaven,
	And gates of triple brass, 
		Siege - proof as portals welded by the Cyclops. 	12
One day Omartes, in his pride of heart,
Led his High Priest, Teleutias, thro’ his halls,
	And chilled by frigid looks, 
		When counting on warm praise, asked - ‘What is wanting? 	14
‘Where is beheld the palace of a king,
So stored with all that doth a king beseem;
	The woofs of Phrygian looms,
		The gold of Colchis, and the pearls of Ormus,         	20
‘Couches of ivory sent from farthest Ind,
Sidonian crystal, and Corinthian Bronze,
	Egypt’s vast symbol gods,
		And those imagined into men by Hellas;	24
‘Stored not in tents that tremble to a gale,
But chambers firm - based, as the Pyramids,
	And breaking into spray
		The surge of Time, as Gades breaks the ocean?’      	28
Nor thou nor I the worth of these things now
Can judge ; we stand too near them; said the sage. 
	None till they reach the tomb
		Scan with just eye the treasures of the palace. 	32
But for the building - as we speak, I feel 
Thro’ all the crannies pierce an icy wind 
	More bitter than the blasts 
		Which howled without the tents of thy rude fathers. 	36
Thou hast forgot to bid thy masons close 
The chinks of stone against Calamity.’ 
	The sage inclined his brow, 
		Shivered, and, parting, round him wrapt his mantle. 	40
The king turned, thoughtful, to a favourite chief, 
The rudest champion of the polished change 
	That fixed the wain - borne homes 
		Of the wild Scythian, and encamped a city; 	44
‘Heard’st thou the Sage, brave Seuthes ?’ asked the King. 
Yea, the priest deemed thy treasures insecure, 
	And fain would see them safe In his own temple;’ 
		The King smiled on Seuthes.    	48
Unto this Scythian monarch’s nuptial bed 
	But one fair girl, Argiope was born: 
	For whom no earthly throne 
		Soared from the level of his fond ambition. 	52
To her, indeed, had Aphrodite given 
	Beauty, that royalty which subjects kings, 
	Sweet with unconscious charm, 
		And modest as the youngest of the Graces. 	56
Men blest her when she moved before their eyes 
	Shame - faced, as blushing to be born so fair,
	Mild as that child of gods
		Violet - crowned Athens hallowing named ‘Pity,’*	60
Now, of a sudden, over that bright face 
	There fell the shadow of some troubled thought,
	As cloud, from purest dews
		Updrawn, makes sorrowful a star in heaven: 	64 
And as a nightingale that having heard 
	A perfect music from some master’s lyre,
	Steals into coverts lone, 
		With her own - melodies no more contented,	68
But haunted by the strain, till then unknown, 
	Seeks to re - sing it back, herself to charm.
	Seeks still and ever fails,
		Missing the key - note which unlocks the music,-	72 
So, from her former pastimes in the choir 
	Of comrade virgins, stole Argiope,
	Lone amid summer leaves
		Brooding that thought which was her joy and trouble. 	76
The King discerned the change in his fair child, 
	And questioned oft, yet could not learn the cause; 
	The sunny bridge between
		The lip and heart which childhood builds was broken. 	80
Not more Aurora, stealing into heaven, 
	Conceals the mystic treasures of the deep
	Whence with chaste blush she comes,
		Than virgin bosoms guard their earliest secret.	84
Omartes sought the priest, to whose wise heart			So dear the maiden, he was wont to say
	That grains of crackling salt 
	From her pure hand, upon the altar sprinkled,         	88
Sent up a flame to loftier heights in heaven
	Than that which rolled from hecatombs in smoke.
	‘King,’ said the musing seer, 
		‘Behold, the woodbine, opening infant blossoms,     	92
‘Perfumes the bank whose herbage hems it round,
	From its own birthplace drinking in delight;
	Later, its instinct stirs; 
		Fain would it climb - to climb forbidden, creepeth,  	96
‘Its lot obeys its yearning to entwine;
	Around the oak it weaves a world of flowers;
	Or, listless drooping, trails 
		Dejected tendrils lost mid weed and briar. 	100
‘There needs no construing to my parable:
	As is the woodbine’s, so the woman’s life:
	Look round the forest kings, 
		And to the stateliest wed thy royal blossom.’         	104
Sharp is a father’s pang when comes the hour
	In which his love contents his child no more,
	And the sweet wonted smile 
		Fades from his hearthstone to rejoice a stranger’s.   	108
But soon from parent love dies thought of self; 
	Omartes, looking round the Lords of earth,
	In young Zariades
		Singled the worthiest of his peerless daughter;       	112
Scion of that illustrious hero - stem, 
	Which in great cyrus bore the loftiest flower 
	Purpled by Orient suns; 
		Stretched his vast satrapies, engulphing kingdoms, 	116
From tranquil palmgroves fringing Caspian waves,
	To the bleak marge of stormy Tanais; 
	On Scythia bordering thus, 
		No foe so dread, and no ally so potent 	120
Perilous boundary - rights by Media claimed 
	O’er that great stream which, laving Scythian plains,
	Europe from Asia guards, 
		The Persian Prince, in wedding Scythia’s daughter	 124
Might well resign, in pledge of lasting peace 
	But ill the project of Omartes pleased 
	His- warlike free - born chiefs,
		And ill the wilder tribes of his fierce people; 	128
For Scyth and Mede had long been as those winds
	Whose very meeting in itself is storm,
	Yet the King’s will prevailed,
		Confirmed, when wavering, by his trusted Seuthes.	132
He, the fierce leader of the fiercest horde,
	Won from the wild by greed of gain and power,
	Stood on the bound between
		Man social and man savage, dark and massive :     	136
So rugged was he that men deemed him true,
	So secret was he that men deemed him wise,
	And he had grown so great,
		The throne was lost behind the subject’s shadow.	140
In the advice he whispered to the king 
	He laid the key - stone of ambitious hope,
	This marriage with the Mede
		Would leave to heirs remote the Scythian kingdom, 	144 
Sow in men’s minds vague fears of foreign rule,
	Which might, if cultured, spring to armed revolt.
	In armed revolt how oft 
		Kings disappear, and none dare call it murder.       	148
And when a crown falls bloodstained in the dust,
	The strong man standing nearest to its fall 
	Takes it and crowns himself; 
		And heirs remote are swept from earth as rebels.     	152
Of peace and marriage-rites thus dreamed the king;
	Of graves and thrones the traitor ; while the fume 
	From altars, loud with prayer 
		To speed the Scythian envoys, darkened heaven.     	156
A hardy prince was young Zariades,
	Scorning the luxuries of the loose - robed Mede,
	Cast in the antique mould 
		Of men whose teaching thewed the sould of Cyrus. 	160
To ride, to draw the bow, to speak the truth, 
	Sufficed to Cyrus,’ said the prince, when child. 
	‘Astyages knew more’
		Answered the Magi - ‘Yes, and lost his kingdoms.’ 	164
Yet there was in this prince the eager mind 
	Which needs must think, and therefore needs must learn; 
	Natures, whose roots strike deep, 
		Clear their own way, and win to light in growing.    	168
His that rare beauty which both charms and awes
	The popular eye ; his the life-gladdening smile;
	His the death - dooming frown; 
		That which he would he could; men loved and feared him. 172
Now of a sudden over this grand brow
	There fell the gloom of some unquiet thought, 
	As when the south wind sweeps 
		Sunshine from Hadria in a noon of summer: 	176
And as a stag, supreme among the herd, 
	With lifted crest inhaling lusty air,
	Smit by a shaft from far,
		Deserts his lordly range amidst the pasture, 	180
And thro’ dim woodlands with drooped antlers creeps
	To the cool marge of rush-grown watersprings; 
	So from all former sports, 
		Contest, or converse with once-loved companions, 	184
Stole the young prince thro’ unfrequented groves, 
	To gaze with listless eyes on lonely streams. 
	All, wondering, marked the change, 
		None dared to question; he had no fond father.    	188
Now, in the thick of this his altered mood, 
	Arrived the envoys of the Scythian king. 
	Reluctant audience found, 
		And spoke to ears displeased their sovereign’s message.	192
‘Omartes greets Zariades the Mede:
	Between the realms of both there rolls a river 
	Inviolate to the Scyth, 
		Free to no keels but those the Scythian charters:	196
Yet have thy subjects outraged oft its waves.
	And pirate foray on our northern banks 
	Ravaged the flocks and herds, 
		Till Scythian riders ask “Why sleeps the Ruler?”	200
‘Still, loth to fan the sparks which leap to flame
	Reddening the nations, from the breath of kings;
	We have not sought thy throne 
		With tales of injury or appeals to justice; 	204
‘But searching in our inmost heart to find 
	The gentlest bond wherewith to link our realms, 
	Make scyth and Mede akin, 
		By household ties their royal chiefs uniting, 	208
We strip our crown of its most precious gem, 
	Proffering to thee our child Argiope:
	So let the Median Queen 
		Be the mild guardian of the Scythian river. 	211
Lifting his brow, replied Zariades:
	Great rivers are the highways of the world: 
	The Tanais laves my shores;
		For those who dwell upon my shores I claim it.      	216
If pirates land on either side for prey,
	My banks grow herdsmen who can guard their herds;
	Take, in these words, reply
		To all complaints that threaten Median subjects.  	  220
But for the gentler phrase wherewith your king 
	Stoops to a proffer, yet implies command, 
	I pray you, in return, 
		To give such thanks as soften most refusal. 	224
‘Thanks are a language kings are born to hear,
	But speak not glibly till they near their fall,
	To guard his Scythian realm,
		On the Mede’s throne the Scyth would place his daughter;	228
‘I should deceive him if I said “Agreed.:”
	No throne, methinks, hath room for  more than one;
	Where a Queen’s lips decide
		or peace or war, she slays the king her husband.	232
‘Thus thinking, did I wed this Scythian maid,
	It were no marriage between Mede and Scyth;
	Nor wrong I unseen charms;
		Love, we are told, comes like the wind from heaven.	236
‘Not at our bidding, but its won free will.
	And so depart - and pardon my plain speech.
	That which I think I say,
		Offending oft - times, but deceiving never’.	240.
So he dismissed them, if with churlish words,
	With royal present, and to festal pomps,
	But one, by Median law
		Nearest his throne, the chief priest of the Magi,	244
Having heard all with not unprescient fears,
	Followed the Prince and urged recall of words
	Which, sent from king to king,
		Are fraught with dragon seeds, whose growth is armies.	248
Mute, as if musing in himself, the Prince
	Heard the wise counsel to its warning close.
	Then, with a gloomy look,
		Gazed on the reader of the stars, and answered	252
Leave thou to me that which to me belongs; 
	My people need the Tanais for their rafts; 
	Or soon or late that need 
		Strings the Mede’s bow, and mounts the Scythian rider. 	256
Mage, I would pluck my spirit from the hold 
	Of a strong phantasy, which, night and day, 
	Haunts it, unsinews life, 
		And makes my heart the foe of my own reason.     	260
Perchance in war, the gods ordain my cure; 
	And courting war, I to myself give peace.
	Startled by these wild words, 
		The Mage, in trust-alluring arts long - practised,         	264
Led on the Prince to unfold their hidden sense; 
	And having bound the listener by the oath 
	Mage never broke, to hold
		Sacred the trust, the King thus told his trouble.     	  268
Know that each night (thro’ three revolving moons) 
	An image comes before me in a dream;
	Ever the same sweet face,
		Lovely as that which blest the Carian’s slumber.    	272
Nought mid the dark - eyed daughters of the East,
	Nought I have ever seen in waking hours,
	Rivals in charm this shape 
		Which hath no life - unless a dream hath substance. 	276
‘But never yet so clearly visible, 
	Nor with such joy in its celestial smile 
	Hath come the visitant, 
		Making a temple of the soul it hallows, 	280
As in the last night’s vision; there it stooped 
	Over my brow, with tresses that I touched.
	With love in bashful eyes, 
		With breath whose fragrance lingered yet in waking, 	284
And balmed the morn, as when a dove, that brings 
	Ambrosia to Olympus, sheds on earth
	Drops from a passing wing: 
		Surely the vision made itself thus living 	288
To test my boast, that truth so fills this soul 
	It could not lodge a falsehood ev’n in dream: 
	Wonderest thou, Magian, now, 
		Why I refuse to wed the Scythian’s daughter ?     	292
And if I thus confide to thee a tale 
	I would not whisper into ears profane, 
	Tis that where reason ends, 
		Men have no choice between the Gods and Chaos. 	296
Ye Magi are the readers of the stars, 
	Versed in the language of the world of dreams: 
	Wherefore consult thy lore, 
		And tell me if Earth hold a mortal maiden 	300
In whom my nightly vision breathes and moves.
	If not, make mine such talismans and spells,
	As banish from the soul 
		Dreams that annul its longing for the daylight.       	304
Up to his lofty fire-tower climbed the mage,

	Thrid the drak maze of books
		Opening on voids beyond the bounds of Nature; 	308

		The dream, adjured against itself to witness, 	312
Refused to. wander from the gate of horn,

	Foiled of diviner lore 
		The Mage resumed his wisdom as a mortal; 	316
And since no Mage can own his science fails, 
	But where that solves not, still solution finds,

		Grave-browed as one whose brain holds Truth new - captured:320
Saying, ‘O King, the shape thy dreams have glassed

	When, on her dragon car, 
		From faithless Jason rose subline Medea, 	324
Refuge at Athens she with Aegeus found;

		And if that form no earthly shape resembles         	328

	Retaining yet dread powers 
		(For memories die not) of her ancient magic, 	332

	Thus, hovering over thee, 
		She warms they heart to love in her - those children. 	336

		And deck the altars where her Medus worshipped; 	340

		And in Zariades revive a Medus.	348
‘Friend,’ sighed the King, ‘albeit I needs must own

	Yet when a young man dreams
		Of decking altars, ’tis not for Medea,	352
He said and turned to lose himself in groves,

		And that same day went back the Scythian envoys.	356

	The “Scythian hosts had crossed the Tanais. 
	And, where the dreamer dreamed, 
		An angry King surveyed his Asian armies. 	368
Who first in fault, the Scythian or the Mede, 
	Who first broke compact, or transgressed a bound, 
	Historic scrolls dispute  
		As Scyth or Mede interprets dreams in story. 	372
Enough for war when two brave nations touch, 
	With rancour simmering in the hearts of kings:
	War is the child of cloud
		Oftentimes stillest just before the thunder. 	376
The armies met in that vast plain whereon 
	The Chaldee, meting out the earth, became 
	The scholar of the stars, - 
		A tombless plain, yet has it buried empires. 	380
At first the Scythian horsemen, right to left,

	But in the central host 
		Stood Persia’s sons, the mountain race of Cyrus;   	384
And in their midst, erect in golden car
	With looks of scorn, Zariades the King;
	And at his trumpet voice 
		Steed felt as man that now began the battle. 	388
Up, sons of Persia, Median women fly; 
	And leave the field to us whom gods made men: 
	The Scythian chases well 
	Yon timorous deer; how let him front the lions.    	392
He spoke, and light - touched by his charioteer

		Following that car as after lightning follow 	396
The hail and whirlwind of collected storm: 
	The Scyths had scattered their own force in chase, 
	As torrents split in rills 
		The giant waves whose gathered might were deluge; 	400

		Fighting on foot; alert in raid or skirmish, 	404

	Foremost himself had rode 
		Heading the charge by which the Medes were scattered;	412

		And thro’ the cloud the ruins of an army!	416
At sunset, sole king on that plain, reigned Death. 
	Far off, the dust-cloud rolled; far off, behind

		The hunted and the hunter, Flight and Havoc	420
‘With the scant remnant of his mighty host

	With words of holy charm,
		Soothing despair and leaving resignation,	452

		Stole from her lips a murmur like complaint,	460

	Now came with stony eye

		And unendurable to Europe’s Freemen;	468

And the Scyth answered - ‘Let the Mede demand

	The water and the soil 
		Are, every grain and every drop, my country’s:       	480
And no man hath a country where a King,

	And at this stern reply, 
		The Persian doomed to fire and sword the city.       	484
Omartes stood within his palace hall,

		‘Did thy prophetic mind rebuke vain - glory. 	488

		With soothing voice,- the hierarch. Calm and tempest 	492
‘Follow .each other in the outward world, 
	And joy and sorrow in the heart of man:
	Wherefore take comfort now,
		The earth and water of the Scyth are grateful,         	496
‘And as thou hast, inviolate to the Scyth, 
	His country saved, that country yet to thee 
	Stretches out chainless arms, 
		And for these walls gives plains that mock besiegers, 	500
Traversed by no invader save the storm,

	Beneath these regal halls,
		Know that there lies a road which leads to safety. 	504
‘For, not unpresicent of the present ills 
	When rose thy towers, the neighbours of the cloud, 
	I, like the mole, beneath, 
		Worked path secure against cloud - riving thunder.   	508
‘Employing Aethiops skilled not in our tongue, 
	Held day and night in the dark pass they hewed:
	And the work done, sent home:
		So the dumb earthworm shares alone the secret.     	512
‘Lo, upon one side ends the unguessed road
	There, - its door panelled in yon far recess,
	Where, on great days of state,
		Oft has thy throne been set beneath the purple;      	5l6
‘The outward issue opes beyond the camp,
